⚠️ Two visuals, both bridges. The paired block comparison is the last piece of world theory in this file; the causal chain below it is the first piece of Indian structure. The lesson exists because the 2026 demand sits exactly on the join.

### Bridge A — Isostasy: why anything stands above anything else

⚠️ **Isostasy** is the buoyant equilibrium between the lighter crust and the denser substratum beneath it: crustal blocks "float" and adjust vertically until they are supported. ⚠️ The physical premise was installed at Lesson 1 — a lighter, granitic **sial** standing on a denser, basaltic **sima**.

⚠️ There are **two conceptions**, and this edition keeps them apart rather than merging them:

- **Airy.** Columns have **similar density** but **different root depth**. A higher mountain has a **deeper root**, exactly as a taller iceberg has more ice below the waterline.
- **Pratt.** Columns rise to **different heights** because their **density differs** above a **common depth of compensation**. A column stands higher because it is lighter, not because it is more deeply anchored.

❗ **Why the distinction must survive.** The familiar argument that the Himalaya must possess a deep crustal root is an **Airy** argument; it has no force under a Pratt conception, where the same height would be explained by low density instead. An answer that writes "the mountains float, so they have roots" has assumed one of the two models without naming it. Writing "on the Airy conception the Himalayan elevation implies a deep root" costs four extra words and converts an assumption into an argument.

⚠️ **Isostatic adjustment is observable**, which is what keeps the concept from being speculative. Removal of a heavy ice load produces long-continued uplift of the deglaciated region — post-glacial rebound. Heavy sediment loading of a delta produces subsidence, which is precisely why deltas are so exposed to relative sea-level rise. 🔁 The deltaic consequence belongs to the coastal-landform owner and is routed here, not developed.

⚠️ **Orogeny** is the mountain-building process itself: sediment accumulation in a subsiding basin, compression at a converging margin, folding, thrusting and crustal thickening, then **isostatic uplift** and prolonged **denudation**. Note the last two terms, because they are what connect this lesson to Lesson 5: a belt does not simply rise once. It is thickened, then buoyed up as it is eroded, and that is why Caledonian and Hercynian belts survive as low rounded uplands rather than disappearing.

### Bridge B — The three-fold relief hierarchy, which is the world's answer and India's question

⚠️ The standard hierarchy that follows from orogeny plus time has three terms:

| Term | Character | Seismicity | Mineral association | Indian instance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Ancient shield** | Stable, worn down, crystalline | Essentially quiet in the sense a fold belt is active | **Mineral-rich** | 🇮🇳 The Peninsular block |
| **Old block or relict mountain** | Rounded, non-seismic, often fault-bounded | Quiet | Variable | 🇮🇳 The Aravali; the Satpura horst |
| **Young fold mountain** | High, sharp, still rising | **Seismically active** | Poor in metallic ores; rich in Tertiary sedimentary resources | 🇮🇳 The Himalaya |

✅ The claim that **shields are the oldest and most stable** portions of the plates is independently stated in the world-half source, and ✅ the Core owner's own three-fold hierarchy is reproduced above. ⚠️ India is therefore not an exception to the world scheme; it is an unusually complete **instance** of it, containing all three terms inside one political boundary.

### Bridge C — India's three geological regions, and the classification contest inside them

✅ Khullar divides India into **three geological regions**:

1. ✅ **The Peninsular region**, which explicitly **includes the Meghalaya plateau and the Kuchchh-Kathiawar region**.
2. ✅ **The extra-Peninsular region** — the Himalayas and their eastern extensions, **including the Andaman and Nicobar Islands**.
3. ✅ **The Indo-Gangetic plain** lying between them.

✅ **And a live disagreement is recorded in the same place: some scholars recognise only two divisions**, a Peninsular block against an Extra-Peninsular region that includes the plain.

⚠️ **Use the disagreement.** A named, citable disagreement is worth more in a Mains answer than a confidently asserted single scheme, because it shows that the candidate knows a division is an analytical decision. The two-division scheme has a real argument behind it: the plain is a **downwarp of the Himalayan foreland**, formed concomitantly with the rise of the range, so it is arguably part of the extra-Peninsular system rather than a third thing. ⚠️ The three-division scheme replies that the plain's **surface material, its age and its land use** are so distinct that treating it as an appendix of the mountains obscures more than it clarifies. Both positions are defensible; the edition teaches the three-fold division and names the alternative.

### Bridge D — The Peninsular block, in the exact terms the 2026 demand needs

✅ Khullar's characterisation is quotable almost in full, and it is in substance an answer spine:

- ✅ **"The Indian Peninsula has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian period."**
- ✅ It is **one of the oldest land-masses**, with an **open senile topography**, and has been **little affected by tectonic forces**.
- ✅ The extra-Peninsula, by contrast, **originated in the Tethys Sea**, carries **the most youthful relief on earth**, is **prone to devastating earthquakes**, and is **"a weak and flexible portion of the earth's crust which has been folded, faulted and overthrust."**
- ✅ The **Indo-Gangetic plain** between the two is **flat, featureless and monotonous**.

> 🔑 **Trap, and it is the precise trap the 2026 demand sets.** ❌ "The Peninsula has never been submerged." ✅ The claim is that it has not undergone marine submergence **since the Cambrian period** — a bounded claim with a date in it. ⚠️ Drop the qualifier and the statement becomes false, because the Peninsula's own Purana and Dharwar sequences include marine deposits from before that boundary, and Lesson 11 shows them. ❗ **Always carry the Cambrian qualifier.**

⚠️ **"Senile" is a technical word, not a disparaging one.** In the cycle-of-erosion vocabulary that Khullar's generation used, a senile surface is one worn down to low relief over a very long period, with subdued slopes and wide open valleys. ⚠️ It implies **prolonged denudation without renewed uplift**, which is exactly what a stable shield undergoes — and it is the formal counterpart of the "most youthful relief on earth" applied to the Himalaya.

### Bridge E — The owned 2026 demand, stated neutrally

🔗 **Linked demand (answer-neutral):** **2026 Prelims GS-I Q34 — the tectonic and geomorphic characteristics of India's Peninsular Block.** ❗ The 2026 Set-A key held locally is **provisional**, and **no answer letter is recorded or inferred** anywhere in this file.

⚠️ What this lesson owes the demand is a short, ordered set of characteristics, each of which is either tectonic or geomorphic and none of which is an option:

| Dimension | The Peninsular Block's characteristic |
|---|---|
| **Age** | Among the oldest land-masses; a stabilised shield of crystalline and metamorphic rock |
| **Marine history** | ✅ **No marine submergence since the Cambrian period** |
| **Tectonic behaviour** | ✅ **Little affected by tectonic forces**; rigid and stable, not a fold belt |
| **Relief** | ✅ **Open senile topography** — low, long-denuded, subdued |
| **Structural style** | Block-faulted uplands, escarpments and rift valleys rather than folded chains |
| **Extent** | ✅ Includes the **Meghalaya plateau** and the **Kuchchh-Kathiawar** region |
| **Lithological consequence** | Crystalline shield hosting metallic minerals; limited primary groundwater, storage in fractures |
| **Contrast term** | Against an extra-Peninsula that is Tethyan in origin, most youthful in relief, earthquake-prone, and folded, faulted and overthrust |

⚠️ Notice that the **Meghalaya plateau** sits inside the Peninsular region although it is physically separated from it by the Ganga-Brahmaputra lowland. ✅ The Advanced owner describes it as a **faulted or detached Peninsular extension**, which is how a geological classification and a physiographic map can disagree without either being wrong: geology follows the rock, physiography follows the present surface.

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | Peninsular block | Extra-Peninsula | Indo-Gangetic plain |
|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ancient stabilised shield | **The Tethys Sea** | **Foredeep** between the other two |
| Age of relief | Oldest; **senile** | **Most youthful on earth** | Quaternary surface |
| Tectonic state | **Little affected by tectonic forces** | **Folded, faulted, overthrust**; still rising | Subsiding and filling |
| Seismic character | Stable in the sense a fold belt is not | **Prone to devastating earthquakes** | Derived from its neighbours' behaviour |
| Marine history | ✅ **No submergence since the Cambrian** | Marine until geologically recently | Never marine in the relevant sense |
| Surface | Plateaux, escarpments, rift valleys | Parallel longitudinal ranges and deep gorges | **Flat, featureless, monotonous** |

**Strongest objection.** The three provinces are standard school geography, and the 2026 question is an objective one. Why does this material need a full bridge lesson?

**Reply.** Because the three provinces are routinely taught as **three facts** and they are **one causal story**. The collision that raised the Himalaya created the depression in front of it, and the erosion of the rising range filled that depression — so the plain exists *because* the mountains do, and the mountains exist *because* a block that had been stable since the Cambrian was driven north into Eurasia. ⚠️ Writing the three divisions as a chain is what converts a description into an explanation, and that conversion is worth more marks in a Mains answer than the division list is worth in an objective one.

⚠️ **Residual.** The number of divisions is itself contested — two or three, as recorded above — and the Peninsula's "stability" is relative rather than absolute: it is stable *in the way a fold belt is not*, which is a comparative statement. 🔁 Intraplate seismicity in the Peninsula is Topic 03's subject and is named here only to prevent the overstatement.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** "Salient features of world's physical geography" (Mains GS-I, direct ownership) for isostasy and the hierarchy; "Indian and World Geography — Physical" (Prelims, direct ownership) for the three provinces.
- **Verified linkage:** **2026 Prelims GS-I Q34 — tectonic and geomorphic characteristics of India's Peninsular Block.** ❗ Provisional key; **answer-neutral**.
- **Probable framing:** in Mains, as the opening structural paragraph of any question on India's relief, drainage, minerals or seismic contrast; the three-province chain is the sentence that frames all four.
- **Traps:** "the Peninsula has never been submerged" without the **Cambrian** qualifier; "senile" read as a judgement rather than as a stage term; Airy and Pratt merged into one flotation metaphor; the plain treated as a depositional accident rather than a **foredeep**; Meghalaya placed outside the Peninsular region because it is physically detached.
- **Answer use:** one sentence carries the whole bridge — *"A block stable since the Cambrian was carried north into Eurasia; the collision raised the most youthful relief on earth and simultaneously created the foredeep that the eroding range then filled."* That is the three provinces, their causal order and their ages in a single clause chain.

Revision notes:

- **Isostasy** = buoyant equilibrium of lighter crust on denser substratum; blocks adjust vertically until supported.
- **Airy:** similar density, **different root depth** — a higher mountain has a **deeper root**. **Pratt:** **different density** above a **common depth of compensation** — a lighter column stands higher.
- ❗ The **Himalayan root argument is an Airy argument**; name the model or the premise is unstated.
- **Isostatic adjustment is observable:** post-glacial rebound after ice unloading; delta subsidence under sediment loading. 🔁 Deltaic vulnerability → coastal owner.
- **Orogeny:** basin sedimentation → compression → folding and thrusting → crustal thickening → **isostatic uplift** → prolonged **denudation**.
- **Three-fold hierarchy:** ancient shields (stable, worn, mineral-rich) · old block/relict mountains (rounded, non-seismic) · young fold mountains (high, sharp, seismic). India holds all three.
- 🇮🇳 **Three geological regions:** Peninsular (**including Meghalaya and Kuchchh-Kathiawar**) · extra-Peninsular (Himalayas and eastern extensions, **including the Andaman and Nicobar Islands**) · the **Indo-Gangetic plain** between.
- ⚠️ **Some scholars recognise only two divisions** — Peninsular block against Extra-Peninsular including the plain. Name the disagreement.
- ✅ **"The Indian Peninsula has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian period."** ❗ The qualifier is the fact.
- ✅ Peninsula: among the oldest land-masses, **open senile topography**, **little affected by tectonic forces**.
- ✅ Extra-Peninsula: originated in the **Tethys Sea**; **most youthful relief on earth**; **prone to devastating earthquakes**; **"folded, faulted and overthrust"**.
- ✅ The plain: **flat, featureless and monotonous**; structurally a **foredeep**.
- **One causal story:** collision raises the range → creates the foredeep in front of it → erosion of the range fills the foredeep.
- 🇮🇳 **Meghalaya** is geologically Peninsular — a **faulted/detached extension** — though physiographically separated by the Ganga-Brahmaputra lowland.

### Concept check

**Question:** Why is it a defensible scholarly position to recognise only two geological divisions of India rather than three, and what is the strongest reply?

**Model answer:** The two-division position treats the Indo-Gangetic plain as part of the extra-Peninsular system rather than as an independent region, and its argument is structural. The plain is not merely an area where sediment happens to lie; its floor is a synclinal downwarp of the Himalayan foreland, formed concomitantly with the rise of the range, and filled by material eroded from that same range. On that reading the plain is the foreland component of the Himalayan orogenic system, and separating them severs a single structure into two. The strongest reply is that classification should track the properties that matter for explanation. The plain's surface material, its Quaternary age, its relief, its groundwater behaviour and its land use are all radically unlike the folded, faulted and overthrust mountain belt, and treating it as an appendix of the mountains obscures everything that makes it geographically distinctive. Both schemes are defensible; what is not defensible is presenting either as the only one.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not read the three-fold division as a statement about three independent origins. It is one causal sequence — collision, uplift, foredeep, infill — and the divisions are its products, not its starting points.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "India's Peninsular block is stable, but stability is a comparative claim rather than an absolute one." Examine. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The stability claim is well founded in its comparative sense. The Peninsula is among the oldest land-masses, a stabilised shield of crystalline and metamorphic rock, little affected by tectonic forces, with an open senile topography produced by prolonged denudation without renewed uplift, and it has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian. Set against the extra-Peninsula — Tethyan in origin, carrying the most youthful relief on earth, folded, faulted and overthrust, and prone to devastating earthquakes — the contrast is absolute in kind. But the term is relational. The Peninsula is stable *in the way a young fold belt is not*; it is traversed by major lineaments, carries block-faulted uplands, escarpments and rift valleys, and the Palghat Gap is probably a rift formed by subsidence between parallel faults. The accurate formulation is a rigid block with inherited structural weaknesses, not an inert one; seismic behaviour belongs to the hazard owner.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the Peninsula characterised with at least three sourced attributes, including the **Cambrian** qualifier; 3 for the comparative contrast with the extra-Peninsula drawn in its own terms; 2 for the qualification that stability is relational, evidenced by faults, rifts or escarpments; 1 for correct use of "senile" as a stage term; 1 for routing seismic hazard rather than annexing it. Deduct 2 for an unqualified "never submerged".

```text
   THE LADDER, BUILT FROM THE GROUND UP   [Khullar's fourfold scheme]

   ------------------------------------------------- unconformity

       (here)      3 depositional cycles; earliest > 3,100 Ma;
                   latest ~2,300 Ma; metamorphosed ~1,000 Ma

   1a. ARCHAEAN    cooling and solidification of the upper crust
       (here)      about 4,000 Ma; all AZOIC; thoroughly

                   'BASEMENT COMPLEX'; gneiss covers about
                   TWO-THIRDS of the Peninsular surface
   =================================================================
   !! WADIA'S OBJECTION cuts across rungs 1a and 1b: the Dharwars may
      be the most ancient metamorphosed SEDIMENTARY system of India,
```

| Gneiss | First identified / named from | Texture and character | Diagnostic association |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bengal Gneiss** | **Midnapur district**; called **Khondolite** in the Eastern Ghats after the **Khond tribes** of Koraput and Bolangir | **Very thinly foliated** | The Eastern Ghats belt |
| **Bundelkhand Gneiss** | Bundelkhand | **Coarse-grained, granite-like**, criss-crossed | Characterised by **quartz veins** |
| **Nilgiri Gneiss** | The Nilgiris | **Bluish-grey to dark plutonic gneiss** of the **Charnockite series** | Named in honour of **Job Charnock**, whose tombstone in Kolkata was made of this rock |

⚠️ Two visuals, one a ladder and one a three-way matrix, because this lesson does two separable things: it installs a chronological frame and it supplies the single most answerable comparison in the Indian half.

### Rung 0 — The scheme, taught as a decision

✅ The Advanced owner locks the frame: **Khullar's older fourfold scheme separates the Archaean, Purana, Dravidian and Aryan rock systems**, after Holland and the Geological Survey of India. ✅ It attaches two explicit warnings, both of which are developed in this lesson and the next: ❌ **do not collapse Dharwar into "all Archaean rocks"**, and ❌ **do not collapse Gondwana into the Dravidian system**.

❗ **And now the divergence, stated openly rather than harmonised.** A second respected Indian volume arranges the same rocks differently. It places the **Aravalli, Dharwar, Cuddapah, Vindhyan, the Meghalaya Plateau and the Mikir Hills all inside an "Archaean System"**, and uses "**the Purana**" as a name for the Pre-Cambrian **Era** rather than for a system of Cuddapah plus Vindhyan, attributing the Indian geological time scale to **T.S. Holland**. Khullar, and the Advanced owner following him, instead make **Purana = Cuddapah + Vindhyan, separate from the Archaean**.

⚠️ **How to hold both.** This edition **teaches Khullar's fourfold scheme** because the Advanced owner locks it, and cites the second volume **for occurrence, etymology and economic detail only**. The divergence itself is examinable material: it demonstrates that the Indian stratigraphic ladder is a **classification decision with consequences**, not a natural fact that the rocks announce. ❗ Silently merging the two schemes would be the easy error and would produce a chronology that contradicts itself at Lesson 11.

⚠️ One further normalisation belongs here. One source states that the term "Archaean" was introduced by **J.D. Dana in 1782**, which cannot be right. ❗ **The date is not used in this edition.** What is used is the undated form: **Dana first applied the term "Archaean" to pre-Cambrian rock structure.**

### Rung 1a — The Archaean, quantified

✅ The Archaean rocks were formed at the **cooling and solidification of the upper crust about 4,000 million years ago**. ✅ They are **all azoic** — entirely without life, and therefore without fossils, which removes one of the four chronology devices of Lesson 4 at the very bottom of the ladder.

✅ Their character: **thoroughly crystalline, extremely contorted and faulted, with plutonic intrusions and a well-defined foliated structure**. ✅ They are known as the **'Fundamental Complex'** or **'Basement Complex'**, which is a functional name rather than a descriptive one: everything above rests on them.

✅ The measurement that most answers need: **gneiss covers about two-thirds of the Peninsular surface**, varying in composition from **granite to gabbro**. ✅ The schists include **mica, talc, hornblende, chlorite, epidote, sillimanite and graphite** varieties. ✅ In the extra-Peninsula the Archaean rocks are exposed all along the Himalayas, **forming the backbone of the mountain system**.

⚠️ The last point is the one that catches candidates out. The Himalaya is a young belt, but it is **not made only of young rock**: it has Archaean material in its core, which is exactly why the Advanced owner says the Himalaya contains **sedimentary, metamorphic and crystalline** units rather than "only young Tertiary sedimentary strata".

✅ One proportion is worth more than a time-scale table: **Precambrian time is approximately 86.7 per cent of Earth's history**, running from an Earth of about 4.6 Ga to the Cambrian at about 570 Ma. ⚠️ Deploy that single figure and you have made the point that an era-by-era table makes at ten times the length.

### Rung 1b — Dharwar, and the objection built into it

✅ The **Dharwar system is named from rocks first studied in Dharwar district, Karnataka**. ✅ It was deposited in **three major cycles**: the earliest **more than 3,100 Ma**, the latest **about 2,300 Ma**, with metamorphism about **1,000 Ma**.

✅ **Economically these are the most important rocks** of the group, carrying **high-grade iron ore, manganese, copper, lead, gold, quartzites, slates and mica**. ✅ The Advanced owner states the same association compactly: the **Archaean and Dharwar crystallines form the Peninsular shield and host most metallic minerals — iron, manganese, gold**, with Kolar named for gold.

✅ **Occurrence**, compressed rather than transcribed: the **Dharwar-Bellary-Mysore** belt; **Ranchi and Hazaribagh**; **Balaghat and Rewa**; **Bastar, Dantewara and Kanker**; **Sundergarh and Keonjhar**; the **Aravallis between Jaipur and Palanpur**; and the **Rialo (Delhi) series running from Delhi to south of Alwar**, which yields the **Makrana marble**. ⚠️ Modern state names are used here where the source used older ones.

✅ **Wadia's objection, and it is not a footnote.** Wadia holds the Dharwars to be the **most ancient metamorphosed sedimentary system of India**, and **as old as, and in some cases older than, the basement gneisses and schists**.

❗ **This is a direct objection to the ladder as a ladder.** If the Dharwars may be older than the basement they are supposed to rest on, then "Archaean first, Dharwar second" is a classification convenience rather than an established sequence. ⚠️ An edition that presents the ladder without this objection teaches a falsehood by omission, and a candidate who writes *"the Dharwars are conventionally placed above the basement gneisses, although Wadia held them to be as old as or older"* has demonstrated exactly the control that separates a high script from a correct one.

> 🔑 **Trap, stated by the Advanced owner in terms.** ❌ Collapsing **Dharwar into "all Archaean rocks"**. ⚠️ The Dharwar is a distinct, **metamorphosed supracrustal sequence** with its own depositional cycles, its own dates and its own mineral association; the Archaean basement is the gneiss-granite complex beneath and around it. Treating the two as one name destroys the distinction on which the Peninsula's entire metallic-mineral geography rests.

### Rung 1c — The mineral consequence, and the one routed current sentence

✅ Archaean rocks are described as the **"repositories of the mineral wealth of India"**, and the accompanying figure carries a **sixteen-mineral legend**. ⚠️ Read with the Dharwar association above, the structural claim is simple and powerful: **India's metallic minerals are where its oldest crystalline rocks outcrop**, which is east-central and southern Peninsular India and not the Gangetic plain.

📰 **One routed sentence, and one only.** The **National Critical Mineral Mission** received Cabinet approval on **29 January 2025**, with an outlay of **₹16,300 crore over seven years (FY 2024-25 to 2030-31)**, an expected **further ~₹18,000 crore** from public-sector and other investment, **1,200** Geological Survey of India critical-mineral exploration projects across that period, and **30** critical minerals identified. 🔁 **That is a mining and critical-minerals policy instrument, and it is not taught here**; its substantive owners are the mineral-and-energy-resources topic and the science-and-technology folder. Topic 02's legitimate interest is the single geological sentence: **India's critical-mineral strategy is constrained by where the Archaean and Dharwar systems actually outcrop.**

❗ The discipline is the point. Naming a mission, dating it, and routing it is a complete and correct treatment. Developing it would be an ownership breach, and an answer that spends a paragraph on mission architecture in a geological-structure question has misallocated its marks.

### Rung 1d — Mapwork for this rung

⚠️ Two map assets belong to this lesson and both are built, not merely viewed.

- **The Archaean distribution overlay** is the **first layer** of the composite geological map taught at Lesson 15. Plot the gneiss-granite basement and the charnockitic tracts first, because everything later is drawn **onto** them.
- **The Archaean formations map with its sixteen-mineral legend** is the asset that converts the lithological layer into a resource layer. ⚠️ Plot the rock first and the minerals second; a learner who memorises the mineral map without the rock map has learnt a distribution without its cause.

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | Archaean (basement) | Dharwar |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | **Cooling and solidification of the upper crust**, about **4,000 Ma** | **Metamorphosed sedimentary** sequence in three depositional cycles |
| Fossils | ✅ **All azoic** | Effectively absent |
| Dates | ~4,000 Ma | Earliest **> 3,100 Ma**; latest **~2,300 Ma**; metamorphosed **~1,000 Ma** |
| Character | Thoroughly crystalline, contorted, faulted, foliated, with plutonic intrusions | Metamorphosed supracrustal rocks: quartzites, slates, schists |
| Extent | ✅ **Gneiss over about two-thirds of the Peninsular surface** | Belts within and upon the basement |
| Economic weight | "Repositories of the mineral wealth of India" | ✅ **Economically the most important rocks**: iron ore, manganese, copper, lead, gold, mica |
| The complication | — | ✅ **Wadia: as old as, or older than, the basement gneisses** |

**Strongest objection.** If the two sources disagree about where the Dharwar belongs, and Wadia disagrees about whether it is younger than the basement at all, why teach any ladder? Why not simply list the systems?

**Reply.** Because a list cannot be reasoned with. The ladder does three things a list cannot: it makes **unconformities meaningful**, since a break has to be a break between two things; it makes the **mineral geography explicable**, because metallic ores associate with the lower rungs and fuels with the upper ones; and it makes the 2026 demand answerable, because "the Peninsula has not been submerged since the Cambrian" is a statement about **position in the ladder**. ⚠️ The correct response to disagreement is not to abandon the frame but to **name it as a frame** and to carry the dissent inside it — which is what this lesson does.

⚠️ **Residual.** Precambrian dating in the Indian literature rests on measurements made at various times by various methods, and the figures quoted here are **the published figures of the cited authors**, not independent determinations. ❗ Quote them with the author or the source attached, and treat the brackets as wide.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** "Indian and World Geography — Physical" (Prelims, direct ownership); supports the GS-I physical-geography clause through the shield-and-mineral argument.
- **Verified linkage:** supports **2026 Prelims GS-I Q34** (Peninsular Block) by supplying the lower rungs of the ladder on which the Cambrian claim rests. ❗ Provisional key; **answer-neutral**.
- **Probable framing:** in Mains, inside a minerals-and-geology question — but ❗ note that such questions are **Topic 31's**, and this lesson supplies the substrate and routes the economics.
- **Traps:** Dharwar collapsed into "all Archaean rocks"; the Himalaya described as containing only young rock; the "Archaean 1782" date reproduced; the two rival schemes merged; Precambrian dates quoted without attribution; the critical-minerals mission developed rather than routed.
- **Answer use:** the sentence that does the most work is *"Gneiss of the Archaean basement covers about two-thirds of the Peninsular surface, and the metamorphosed Dharwar sequences upon it are economically the most important rocks in India, carrying high-grade iron ore, manganese, copper, lead and gold."* It is a lithological claim and a resource claim at once.

Revision notes:

- **Fourfold scheme (Holland / GSI, as locked by the Advanced owner):** **Archaean → Purana → Dravidian → Aryan**.
- ❗ **Scheme divergence:** a second source places **Aravalli, Dharwar, Cuddapah, Vindhyan, Meghalaya Plateau and Mikir Hills inside an "Archaean System"** and uses "Purana" for the Pre-Cambrian **Era**. Name the divergence; do not harmonise it.
- ❗ Dana applied the term **"Archaean"** to pre-Cambrian rock structure — **no date is used**, because the one printed locally is impossible.
- **Archaean:** formed at the **cooling and solidification of the upper crust, ~4,000 Ma**; **all azoic**; thoroughly crystalline, contorted, faulted, foliated, with plutonic intrusions; the **'Fundamental Complex' / 'Basement Complex'**.
- ✅ **Gneiss covers about two-thirds of the Peninsular surface**, from **granite to gabbro** in composition; schists include **mica, talc, hornblende, chlorite, epidote, sillimanite, graphite**.
- ✅ In the extra-Peninsula, Archaean rocks are exposed all along the Himalayas, **forming the backbone of the mountain system** — so the young belt is not made only of young rock.
- **Precambrian ≈ 86.7 per cent of Earth's history** (≈4.6 Ga to ≈570 Ma). One proportion replaces a table.
- **Dharwar:** named from **Dharwar district, Karnataka**; **three depositional cycles**; earliest **> 3,100 Ma**, latest **~2,300 Ma**, metamorphosed **~1,000 Ma**.
- ✅ **Economically the most important rocks:** high-grade **iron ore, manganese, copper, lead, gold, quartzites, slates, mica**; **Kolar** for gold.
- **Occurrence:** Dharwar-Bellary-Mysore · Ranchi and Hazaribagh · Balaghat and Rewa · Bastar, Dantewara, Kanker · Sundergarh and Keonjhar · Aravallis between Jaipur and Palanpur · **Rialo (Delhi) series, Delhi to south of Alwar → Makrana marble**.
- ❗ **Wadia:** the Dharwars are the most ancient metamorphosed **sedimentary** system of India, **as old as, and in some cases older than, the basement gneisses and schists**.
- ❌ **Do not collapse Dharwar into "all Archaean rocks."** Distinct supracrustal sequence, distinct dates, distinct mineral association.
- **Three gneisses:** **Bengal** (very thinly foliated; **Midnapur**; **Khondolite** in the Eastern Ghats, from the **Khond** tribes of Koraput and Bolangir) · **Bundelkhand** (coarse, granite-like, **quartz veins**) · **Nilgiri** (bluish-grey to dark, **Charnockite series**, named for **Job Charnock**).
- Archaean rocks = **"repositories of the mineral wealth of India"**; the formations map carries a **sixteen-mineral** legend.
- 📰🔁 **National Critical Mineral Mission, Cabinet approval 29 January 2025**, ₹**16,300 crore** over seven years, ~₹**18,000 crore** further expected, **1,200** GSI projects, **30** critical minerals. **Routed, not taught.** Topic 02's interest is one sentence: the strategy is constrained by **where the Archaean and Dharwar outcrop**.

### Concept check

**Question:** Why does Wadia's view of the Dharwars create a problem for the fourfold scheme, and how should a candidate handle it in an answer?

**Model answer:** The fourfold scheme arranges Indian rocks as an ascending sequence in which the Archaean basement is the lowest and oldest rung and the Dharwar sequences lie upon it. Wadia holds that the Dharwars are the most ancient metamorphosed sedimentary system of India and are as old as, and in some cases older than, the basement gneisses and schists. If that is right, the relation between the two rungs is not a simple superposition, and the ordering becomes a convention of classification rather than a demonstrated stratigraphic sequence. The handling is not to abandon the scheme, because it remains the frame the Advanced owner locks and the frame in which unconformities, mineral associations and the Cambrian claim are stated. The handling is to use the scheme and attach the dissent in one clause — that the Dharwars are conventionally placed above the basement although Wadia regarded them as of comparable or greater antiquity. That converts a recited ladder into a controlled position.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat the two Indian schemes as a question of which author is correct. They classify the same rocks for different purposes; what is wrong is to take occurrence and etymology from one and chronology from the other, which produces a sequence that contradicts itself.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "India's metallic mineral geography is a direct expression of where its oldest rocks are exposed." Examine, and state the limits of the claim. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The association is strong. The Archaean basement, formed at the cooling of the upper crust and covering about two-thirds of the Peninsular surface as gneiss, is described as the repository of India's mineral wealth; and the Dharwar sequences upon it are economically the most important rocks in the country, carrying high-grade iron ore, manganese, copper, lead, gold, quartzites, slates and mica. Their outcrop belts — Dharwar-Bellary-Mysore, Ranchi and Hazaribagh, Bastar, Sundergarh and Keonjhar — are precisely where India's metallic mining is concentrated, while the alluvial plain over the same minerals' latitude has almost none. Three limits apply. The association is probabilistic, not deterministic: outcrop indicates a favourable host, not a deposit. Grade, depth, accessibility and economics decide what is mined. And fuels follow a different rule entirely, since coal is Gondwana and petroleum is Tertiary sedimentary, so the claim holds for metals only.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the Archaean-Dharwar association stated with the mineral list; 2 for at least three named outcrop belts; 2 for the negative case — the alluvial plain — used as the contrast; 2 for the limits, including association against determinism; 1 for the fuel exception correctly separated. Deduct 2 for treating lithology as sufficient for a deposit.

## Lesson 11 — India's ladder II: the Purana basins and the Dravidian gap

Progress: 11 / 15  |  Stage: Advanced  |  Subtopic: Cuddapah and Vindhyan geometry, the great unconformity, and an absence that is itself the examinable fact

━━━ PRE-TEACH CHECKLIST ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Book context: Advanced owner §2 (Purana as Cuddapah and Vindhyan; building stone, limestone, Panna diamonds; Dravidian as Palaeozoic and limited in India); D.R. Khullar, Chapter 2, printed pp.43-45 (Purana geometry and thicknesses; the Vindhyan split; the Dravidian absence; the period-by-period inventory with etymologies); Majid Husain, *Geography of India*, Chapter 1, printed pp.1.6-1.9 (Cuddapah and Vindhyan occurrence; the Great Boundary Fault; the Vindhyan monument association).
CA search: "Vindhyan Cuddapah basin Panna diamond Great Boundary Fault news 2026" — run for this lesson.
CA found: None in last 6 months. Honest nil return; nothing is manufactured for a Proterozoic basin lesson.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

```text
            CROSS-SECTION THROUGH A PURANA BASIN
  ==================================================================
   W                                                              E
   ___________________________________________________________
  |  UPPER VINDHYAN  1,000-600 Ma                               |
  |  FLUVIATILE | gently lying | UNDISTURBED HORIZONTAL STRATA  |
  |  >> encloses TWO DIAMOND-BEARING HORIZONS -> PANNA, GOLCONDA|
  |_____________________________________________________________|
  ~~~~~~~~ unconformity: PROMINENT in the north, ALMOST ABSENT in
           southern Mewar, Chittaurgarh and the Son valley ~~~~~~~~
  |\  LOWER VINDHYAN  1,300-1,100 Ma                           /|
  | \ MARINE | CALCAREOUS | TECTONICALLY FOLDED               / |
  |  \______________________________________________________ /  |
  ============ A G R E A T   U N C O N F O R M I T Y ============
  |  DHARWAR  (L10) -- metamorphosed, deformed, planed off       |
  |______________________________________________________________|
  |  ARCHAEAN BASEMENT  (L10)                                    |
  |______________________________________________________________|

   Purana sediments: unfossiliferous CLAYS, SLATES, QUARTZITES,
   SANDSTONES and LIMESTONES laid in GREAT SYNCLINAL BASINS;
   up to 6,100 m thick in parts of southern India.
```

```text
         THE CUDDAPAH OUTCROP IN PLAN -- a shape that argues
  ==================================================================
                      . . . . . . . .
                 . .                   . .
             . .           INTERIOR         . .
           .          (the CONCAVE side)       .
          .                                     .      ~45,000 sq km
          .     C R E S C E N T                 .      irregular crescent
           .                                   .       CONCAVE TOWARD
             . .                            . .        THE COAST
                 . .                    . .
                      . . . . . . . .
                                        >>>>> C O A S T >>>>>

   THE INFERENCE: a crescent concave toward the coast implies
   COMPRESSIVE FORCES DIRECTED FROM THE CONCAVE SIDE -- where high
   mountains once stood. A map SHAPE carrying a tectonic inference
   is rare; this one is worth a sentence in any structural answer.

   AND THE LINEAMENT THAT BOUNDS THE SYSTEM:
   GREAT BOUNDARY FAULT -- separates the VINDHYAN SYSTEM from the
   ARAVALLIS for about 800 km.
```

⚠️ The section and the plan sketch do different work: one carries **time**, the other carries **stress direction**. ⚠️ Both are drawable in under a minute and both are rare enough in a script to be noticed.

### §A. The name, the span and the geometry

✅ **"Purana" is used in place of "Proterozoic"**, and the system has **two divisions: the Cuddapah System and the Vindhyas**. ✅ The Advanced owner dates the group at **1400-600 million years** and attaches its economic signature: **building stone, limestone and Panna diamonds**.

✅ The sediments are **unfossiliferous clays, slates, quartzites, sandstones and limestones**, deposited in **great synclinal basins**, and they are **separated from the Dharwars by a great unconformity**. ✅ In parts of southern India the sequence reaches **6,100 m** in thickness. ✅ Along the main Aravalli axis, from **Delhi to Idar in Gujarat**, the aggregate is **5,200 m**.

⚠️ Three deductions follow at once, and all three are examinable.

1. **Unfossiliferous** means the fossil device of Lesson 4 is unavailable, so correlation between basins is hard and dates are soft. ❗ This is why Purana ages are given here in wide brackets with their source attached.
2. **Great synclinal basins** means these are **sagging, sediment-receiving structures**, not blanket covers. The rock is thick where the basin was deep and absent where it was not — which is why the outcrop map is a set of patches rather than a sheet.
3. **A great unconformity below** means the Dharwars were deposited, deformed, uplifted and **planed off** before Purana deposition began. The break is the single largest statement about time in the Indian lower ladder.

### §B. The Cuddapah System, and a map shape that argues

✅ The Cuddapah outcrop is an **irregular crescent, concave toward the coast, of about 45,000 sq km**. ✅ The shape *implies compressive forces directed from the concave side, where high mountains once stood*.

⚠️ Pause on that, because it is unusual. Most map shapes are descriptive. This one is **inferential**: the geometry of a basin margin records the direction from which it was pushed, and the push implies a former upland that no longer exists. ⚠️ A candidate who writes *"the crescentic Cuddapah outcrop, concave toward the coast, implies compression from the concave side and therefore a vanished mountain belt in that direction"* has produced a structural argument from a map, which is the skill Lesson 15 is built to train.

### §C. The Vindhyan, split into two tectonically different halves

✅ This is the lesson's highest-value Indian content, because it is almost always taught as one thing and is two.

| | **Lower Vindhyan** | **Upper Vindhyan** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **1,300-1,100 Ma** | **1,000-600 Ma** |
| Depositional environment | **Marine** | **Fluviatile** |
| Composition | **Calcareous** | Sandstones and associated clastics |
| Structural state | **Tectonically folded** | **Gently lying, undisturbed horizontal strata** |
| Separated by | ✅ An unconformity **prominent in the north and almost absent in southern Mewar, Chittaurgarh and the Son valley** | |
| Economic signature | — | ✅ Encloses **two diamond-bearing horizons → Panna and Golconda** |

✅ Otherwise the Vindhyan is **devoid of metalliferous minerals** but yields **durable building stone, flagstones, ornamental stone, limestone, pure glass-making sand and some coal**. ✅ Its extent is **over 1,00,000 sq km**.

❗ **The distinction is tectonic, not a matter of thickness.** Lower = marine, calcareous and folded; Upper = fluviatile, horizontal and undisturbed. ⚠️ That means the interval between them contains a **change of environment and a deformation event**, and the unconformity between them is the record of it — the stratigraphic toolkit of Lesson 4 applied to the most quotable Indian case available.

✅ A nomenclature point that costs nothing and earns something: **Spate distinguished the Vindhyan rocks from the Vindhyan hills.** ⚠️ The rock system and the range are not coextensive; a question about one is not automatically a question about the other.

✅ **The Great Boundary Fault separates the Vindhyan System from the Aravallis for about 800 km.** ⚠️ This is the single named lineament the India half most needs, and it belongs in the lineament sketch at Lesson 15 alongside the **Main Central Thrust** of the extra-Peninsula.

⚠️ **A cross-link, named once and routed.** ✅ Vindhyan red sandstone built the **Qutab Minar, Humayun's Tomb, Fatehpur Sikri, Agra Fort, the Red Fort, Jama Masjid, Birla Mandir and the Sanchi Stupa**. 🔁 That is Art and Culture's property. Name it as a demonstration that a lithology can be read off a monument, and stop.

### §D. The Dravidian gap — an absence that is the fact

✅ The Dravidian system is **Palaeozoic**, and the Advanced owner records it as **limited in India** and **best developed in parts of the extra-Peninsula**.

✅ Khullar is blunter, and the bluntness is the examinable content: Dravidian rocks are **"conspicuous by their absence in the Peninsular India except for one or two small patches of lower Permian age near Umaria"**, and ✅ **Cambrian and Devonian rocks containing recognisable fossils are unknown in the Peninsula**.

⚠️ **Why an absence is worth more than a presence here.** Lesson 9's controlling Peninsular claim is that the block **has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian period**. The Dravidian gap is that claim's *evidence*. If the Peninsula had been under the sea during the Palaeozoic, there would be Palaeozoic marine sediment on it. There is almost none. The absence and the claim are the same fact seen twice, and an answer that joins them has explained rather than asserted.

❗ **And the warning the Advanced owner attaches:** ❌ **do not place the Gondwana system inside the Dravidian.** ⚠️ The stratigraphic reason is exactly the one just given — the Dravidian is substantially **absent** from the Peninsula, while the Gondwana is the Peninsula's defining **Aryan** sequence, taught at Lesson 12. Putting Gondwana in the Dravidian both misdates India's coal and contradicts the Cambrian claim.

### §E. The Dravidian period by period, with etymologies that are memory hooks

✅ Where the Dravidian does occur — chiefly in the extra-Peninsula — the inventory is period by period, and the names carry their own mnemonics.

| Period | Date | Etymology | Indian expression |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Cambrian** | **600 Ma** | After **Cambria**, the Latin name for **Wales** | The **Haimanta System** of Spiti, a **1,600 m** deposit; the **saline series** of the Salt Range — **900 m** of fossiliferous sandstones, shales and dolomites underlain by salt marl |
| **Ordovician** | **500 Ma** | After the **Ordovices**, a tribe of Wales | — |
| **Silurian** | **440 Ma** | After the **Silures**, of the Welsh-English border | A thick continuous band around the core of the **Lidar anticline** |
| **Devonian** | **400 Ma** | After **Devonshire** | Massive white quartzite up to **900 m**, **devoid of fossils** — the **Muth quartzite** of Spiti and Kumaon |
| **Carboniferous** | **350 Ma** | ✅ **"Carboniferous in geology means coal bearing"** | ✅ **Mount Everest is composed of Upper Carboniferous limestones** |

⚠️ The last row is the one to carry into an examination hall. **Everest is built of marine limestone** — rock laid down on a sea floor, now standing at the top of the world. It is the single most vivid demonstration available that the Himalaya is **uplifted sea bed**, and it belongs in any answer about the Tethys at Lesson 13.

⚠️ **Normalisations applied here, declared.** ❗ One source gives the Palaeozoic as extending from 570 Ma to "24.5 million years ago", which is almost certainly a typesetting slip; **the terminal date is not quoted** in this edition, and Khullar's internally consistent period-by-period dates above are used instead. ❗ The same source places the break-up of Pangaea and the origin of the Tethys inside the Palaeozoic; **assembly and break-up are taught here as separate events on opposite sides of the Permo-Triassic boundary**, following the ordered chronology used at Lessons 12 and 13.

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | Purana | Dravidian |
|---|---|---|
| Era | Proterozoic, **1400-600 Ma** | **Palaeozoic** |
| Peninsular presence | ✅ Extensive — Vindhyan alone **over 1,00,000 sq km** | ✅ **Conspicuous by its absence**, except small patches near **Umaria** |
| Fossils | **Unfossiliferous** | Fossiliferous where present; ✅ Cambrian and Devonian fossil-bearing rocks **unknown in the Peninsula** |
| Depositional setting | **Great synclinal basins** | Chiefly extra-Peninsular, Tethyan |
| Economic signature | Building stone, limestone, **Panna diamonds**, glass sand | ✅ Carboniferous = "coal bearing" **as a name**, but not India's coal |
| What it proves about the Peninsula | That basins subsided and received sediment | ✅ That the block **was not submerged** after the Cambrian |

**Strongest objection.** Proterozoic basin geometry is specialist stratigraphy. A General Studies paper will never ask for the thickness of the Cuddapah.

**Reply.** It will not, and this lesson does not ask a candidate to recall 6,100 m under examination pressure. What it asks for are **three transferable moves**. First, read an **unconformity** as a dated event rather than as a line on a diagram. Second, read a **map shape** as a record of stress direction. Third, read an **absence** — the Dravidian gap — as positive evidence for a claim stated elsewhere. ⚠️ All three are reasoning skills, and all three appear in the 2026 demand's territory even though none of them appears in its wording.

⚠️ **Residual.** Purana chronology is **soft**, precisely because the sequences are unfossiliferous. Dates here are the **cited authors'** figures and the brackets are wide; a confident single figure for the base or top of the Vindhyan is not available from the sources this edition uses.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** "Indian and World Geography — Physical" (Prelims, direct ownership).
- **Verified linkage:** supports **2026 Prelims GS-I Q34** by supplying the Dravidian gap, which is the evidence for the Cambrian submergence claim. ❗ Provisional key; **answer-neutral**.
- **Probable framing:** in Mains, as the structural middle of any question on India's sedimentary basins, building materials or the geological basis of a regional contrast.
- **Traps:** Gondwana placed in the Dravidian; the Vindhyan treated as one undivided unit; the Lower/Upper split described as a thickness difference; the Vindhyan **hills** equated with the Vindhyan **rocks**; the Palaeozoic terminal date of "24.5 Ma" reproduced; Pangaea's break-up placed in the Palaeozoic.
- **Answer use:** *"Lower Vindhyan — marine, calcareous, folded; Upper Vindhyan — fluviatile, horizontal, undisturbed; an unconformity between, and two diamond-bearing horizons in the upper division at Panna and Golconda."* Twenty-eight words, four structural facts, one resource fact.

Revision notes:

- **"Purana" replaces "Proterozoic."** Two divisions: **Cuddapah System** and **the Vindhyas**; the Advanced owner dates the group **1400-600 Ma**.
- Sediments: **unfossiliferous** clays, slates, quartzites, sandstones, limestones in **great synclinal basins**; up to **6,100 m** in parts of southern India; **5,200 m** along the Aravalli axis from **Delhi to Idar (Gujarat)**.
- ❗ **Separated from the Dharwars by a great unconformity.**
- **Cuddapah outcrop:** irregular **crescent, concave toward the coast, ~45,000 sq km**; the shape implies **compression from the concave side, where high mountains once stood**.
- **Lower Vindhyan 1,300-1,100 Ma:** **marine, calcareous, tectonically folded**. **Upper Vindhyan 1,000-600 Ma:** **fluviatile, gently lying, undisturbed horizontal strata**.
- The separating unconformity is **prominent in the north, almost absent in southern Mewar, Chittaurgarh and the Son valley**.
- **Upper Vindhyan encloses two diamond-bearing horizons → Panna and Golconda.** Otherwise **devoid of metalliferous minerals**; yields **durable building stone, flagstones, ornamental stone, limestone, pure glass-making sand, some coal**. Extent **over 1,00,000 sq km**.
- ✅ **Spate distinguished the Vindhyan rocks from the Vindhyan hills.**
- ✅ **Great Boundary Fault separates the Vindhyan System from the Aravallis for about 800 km.**
- 🔁 Vindhyan red sandstone built the **Qutab Minar, Humayun's Tomb, Fatehpur Sikri, Agra Fort, Red Fort, Jama Masjid, Birla Mandir, Sanchi Stupa** — named once, routed to Art and Culture.
- **Dravidian = Palaeozoic**, ✅ **"conspicuous by their absence in the Peninsular India except for one or two small patches of lower Permian age near Umaria"**; ✅ fossil-bearing **Cambrian and Devonian rocks are unknown in the Peninsula**.
- ⚠️ **The absence is the evidence** for "no marine submergence since the Cambrian" (L9).
- ❌ **Do not place Gondwana in the Dravidian.** Gondwana is **Aryan** (L12).
- **Period inventory:** **Cambrian 600 Ma** (after **Cambria** = Wales) — **Haimanta System** of Spiti, **1,600 m**; **saline series** of the Salt Range, **900 m** over salt marl · **Ordovician 500 Ma** (**Ordovices**) · **Silurian 440 Ma** (**Silures**) — band around the **Lidar anticline** · **Devonian 400 Ma** (Devonshire) — **Muth quartzite**, up to **900 m**, **devoid of fossils** · **Carboniferous 350 Ma** — **"coal bearing"**; ✅ **Mount Everest is composed of Upper Carboniferous limestones**.
- ❗ Not quoted: the "Palaeozoic to 24.5 Ma" terminal date; Pangaea's break-up placed in the Palaeozoic.

### Concept check

**Question:** Explain how the near-absence of Dravidian rocks in Peninsular India functions as evidence for a claim about the Peninsula made in an entirely different part of the geological argument.

**Model answer:** The claim is that the Indian Peninsula has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian period. That claim predicts a specific observation: if the block stood above sea level throughout the Palaeozoic, then Palaeozoic marine sediment should be largely missing from it. The observation matches. Dravidian rocks are described as conspicuous by their absence in Peninsular India except for one or two small patches of lower Permian age near Umaria, and fossil-bearing Cambrian and Devonian rocks are unknown in the Peninsula; where the Dravidian is well developed is the extra-Peninsula, which was Tethyan sea at the time. So the absence is not a gap in knowledge but a positive datum, and it converts the submergence claim from an assertion into an inference supported by the stratigraphic record. It also enforces the second rule: since the Peninsula's great post-Palaeozoic sequence is the Gondwana, that sequence must be Aryan and cannot be Dravidian.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat a missing system as a failure of preservation by default. Absence can mean non-deposition or it can mean removal by erosion, and here the surrounding evidence — a stable, long-emergent, senile block — points to non-deposition, which is why the absence supports the submergence claim rather than merely being consistent with it.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "The Vindhyan system records a change of environment and a deformation event, not merely a change of rock." Discuss. *(Discuss — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The system divides into two tectonically distinct halves. The Lower Vindhyan, roughly 1,300-1,100 million years, is marine and calcareous and is tectonically folded. The Upper Vindhyan, roughly 1,000-600 million years, is fluviatile and lies in gently disposed, undisturbed horizontal strata. Between them runs an unconformity whose prominence is itself regionally variable, pronounced in the north and almost absent in southern Mewar, Chittaurgarh and the Son valley. Reading the pair structurally, the lower beds were deposited in a sea, then folded, uplifted and planed off; only afterwards were river-laid beds spread flat across their truncated edges. That is an environmental change from marine to fluviatile and a deformation event, recorded in the same surface. The economic expression follows the division, since it is the Upper Vindhyan that encloses the two diamond-bearing horizons at Panna and Golconda. The qualification is that unfossiliferous sequences date poorly, so the brackets are wide.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the Lower/Upper contrast on environment, composition and structural state; 3 for the unconformity read as a sequence of five events rather than as a line; 2 for the regional variability of the break; 1 for the diamond horizons attached to the correct division; 1 for the dating qualification. Deduct 2 for describing the split as a difference of thickness.

## Lesson 12 — The Aryan system I: Gondwana rift basins, the Deccan Trap, and India's coal geography

Progress: 12 / 15  |  Stage: Advanced  |  Subtopic: Loaded troughs on an ancient plateau, a flood-basalt province twice the size of its remnant, and where India's coal actually is
