```text
   THE EVIDENCE LADDER -- what each extra band of marks BUYS
     10 marks  thesis + 3-4 mechanism steps + one Indian example +
               one qualification                      (~150 words)
     15 marks  + a SECOND MODE of evidence: a map/spatial expression
               OR a living-example sequence, AND an evaluation or
               consequence paragraph                  (~250 words)
     20 marks  + a SECOND AXIS: comparison, counter-case or
               governance/economic dimension, closing on a GRADED
               verdict that concedes a real cost      (~250 words)
   >> Extra marks buy a NEW KIND of content, never more of the same.
```

⚠️ This lesson carries **skills, not content**. Everything in it is transferable to any structural question in this folder, and nothing in it is a fact to be recalled for its own sake.

### Instrument 1 — The nine map assets, and which lesson owns each

| # | Asset | What it carries | Owning lesson |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** | **World structural divisions** — Young Alpine Folds, Hercynian Folds, Caledonian Folds, Pre-Cambrian Ancient Shields | The orogenic chronology as a map legend | L5, and the world half of L15 |
| **M2** | **Major and minor plates** | The seven major plates plus several minor ones; boundary types traced around each | L8 |
| **M3** | **India: Geology** — the composite | The sixteen-class legend that is itself a chronology | **L15, taught as a build** |
| **M4** | **Eight single-system distribution overlays** — Archaean, Dharwar, Cuddapah, Vindhyan; Gondwana, Deccan Trap, Tertiary, Pleistocene-and-Recent | One system at a time, in order | L10-L13, assembled at L15 |
| **M5** | **Four-panel palaeogeographic sequence** — Permian, Jurassic, Eocene, Miocene | Drift and collision as a sequence rather than a list | L13 |
| **M6** | **Archaean formations with a mineral legend** | The conversion of a lithological layer into a resource layer | L10 |
| **M7** | **Deccan Trap thickness profile** — Mumbai ~3,000 m, Kachchh 800 m, southern limit 600-800 m, Amarkantak 150 m | The province as a **west-thickening wedge** | L12 |
| **M8** | **Peninsular hill-range inventory** — Aravali, Vindhyan, Satpura, Western Ghats, Eastern Ghats with the three-fold southern nomenclature | Structural typing of relief | L14 |
| **M9** | **Lineament sketch** — the **Great Boundary Fault** and the **Main Central Thrust** | Two named structures, one Peninsular and one extra-Peninsular | L11 and L15 |

⚠️ **The plotting discipline for M3 is the lesson's central instruction.** ✅ The composite geological map carries a legend that is a teachable chronology in itself: Quaternary; Shiwaliks and Tipam Series; Lower and Middle Miocene; Palaeocene; Coastal Tertiary; Rajmahal and Deccan Flows; Mesozoic; Mesozoic and Palaeozoic windows; Gondwana and Upper Palaeozoic; Vindhyan and Cuddapah Systems; Purana and Lower Palaeozoic; **Malani Volcanics**; Dharwar System; Granites of all ages; Unclassified crystallines; **Charnockites**.

❗ **Teach it as a build, never as a finished object.** Shields and granites first, then Dharwar, then Purana, then Gondwana, then Deccan flows, then Tertiary, then Quaternary — the order of the rail above. ⚠️ A learner who can draw the build in that order **has** the chronology; a learner who memorises the finished map has a picture of it.

⚠️ **A second lineament note, carried with its caution.** ✅ A list of Indian **series and faults** is available — Bijwar, Champaner, Champion, Chilpi, Closepet, Damuda, Iron, Kaldagi, Khondolite, Panchet, Rialo, Sakoli, Sausar and Talcher, with the **Main Central Thrust** heading the fault list. ❗ That list was recovered from a page whose two-column layout renders unreliably, so **individual series names from it are not quoted here as decisive**; what is carried forward is the **Main Central Thrust** as the named extra-Peninsular structure and the Great Boundary Fault as the named Peninsular one, both of which are independently corroborated in this file.

### Instrument 2 — Directive decoding, extended

⚠️ The decoding table above is the Core owner's, with two topic-specific rows added by this edition for the demands the owner's table does not cover.

- **"Characteristics and types of primary rocks" (Describe, 10).** The addition is that *types* must be given on **two axes** — acid against basic, and plutonic against volcanic — and that the **forms** (batholith, stock, laccolith, sill, dike, phacolith; flow and fissure sheet) are part of "types". Most scripts give one axis and no forms.
- **"Tectonic and geomorphic characteristics of a block" (objective).** The addition is that *tectonic* and *geomorphic* are **two different lists**: tectonic covers age, stability, deformation history and marine history; geomorphic covers relief, surface form and structural style. ⚠️ A candidate who conflates them will answer only half the demand.

### Instrument 3 — Evidence by marks

⚠️ The ladder above states the rule; here is what it means in this topic specifically.

| Band | What to put in | Worked allocation for this topic |
|---|---|---|
| **10** | Thesis, three or four mechanism steps, one Indian example, one qualification | *Primary rocks*: definition of primary · two-axis types · forms · one Indian case each side · the texture-is-generally-reliable qualification |
| **15** | All of the above **plus** a second mode of evidence **plus** an evaluation or consequence paragraph | *Crustal tectonics*: drift evidence · spreading mechanism · boundary regimes · **the six-stage cycle with living examples** · passive-margin resource consequence · two qualifications |
| **20** | All of the above **plus** a second axis — comparison, counter-case, or governance and economic dimension — closing on a graded verdict | *Structure and resource*: the causal fan · the controlled Gangetic-against-Chotanagpur comparison · the determinism counter-case · the routing of economics to its owner · a verdict that concedes what structure does **not** explain |

❗ **The rule that prevents the commonest misallocation:** extra marks buy a **new kind** of content. A fifteen-mark answer that is a ten-mark answer with longer sentences scores as a ten-mark answer.

### Instrument 4 — The three evidence units, ready to deploy

✅ The Core owner supplies three units in the form **claim → evidence → significance → limitation**, and they are reproduced because each is a complete paragraph that can be inserted into several different answers.

1. ✅ **Claim:** ocean basins have a life cycle rather than a fixed identity. **Evidence:** the East African Rift, Red Sea, Atlantic, Pacific, Mediterranean and Himalayan suture occupy successive stages of one sequence **simultaneously**. **Significance:** it lets an answer *demonstrate* change using places that exist today. **Limitation:** the stages are a conceptual ordering, not a timetable; a basin can stall or reverse.
2. ✅ **Claim:** tectonic setting, not chance, fixes where hydrocarbons and where earthquakes are. **Evidence:** wide passive margins accumulate thick sediment wedges and host offshore oil; active margins carry trenches, arcs and deep-focus seismicity. **Significance:** it links structure directly to resource geography and hazard zoning. **Limitation:** a sedimentary basin is necessary but not sufficient — source, reservoir, seal and trap must all be present.
3. ✅ **Claim:** rock genesis governs the surface geography above it. **Evidence:** the extrusive basalt of the Deccan weathers to black soil underpinning a cotton belt, while crystalline shields store groundwater in fractures rather than pore space. **Significance:** it converts petrology into an agriculture and water-security argument. **Limitation:** soil formation also depends on climate, relief, organisms and time — parent rock alone does not determine soil.

⚠️ A fourth unit, specific to the India half of this edition, follows the same form. **Claim:** India's mineral and agricultural geographies do not coincide, and the reason is lithological. **Evidence:** the alluvial plain is extremely useful for agriculture and almost completely devoid of minerals; the crystalline Peninsula, especially Chotanagpur, is the reverse. **Significance:** it explains, in one comparison, why India's industrial core and its agricultural core are in different places. **Limitation:** lithology fixes where ores **can** occur, not what is extracted; grade, depth, infrastructure, price and policy decide that.

### Instrument 5 — The factual-caution protocol, in three classes

❗ Both canonical owners carry standing cautions, and this edition converts them into a sorting rule to be applied **before** any number is written.

| Class | Rule | Members in this topic |
|---|---|---|
| **A — quote freely** | Structural constants and definitional values | Moho ~35 km · Gutenberg ~2,900 km · section markers 305/700/2,900/5,150/6,371 km · lithosphere 75-125 km · asthenosphere 250-700 km · oceanic crust ~5 km against continental 30-40 km · "seven major plates" as a convention · batholith ≥ 100 sq km, stock < 100 sq km · the six classification criteria · the three sedimentary origin classes · the five drift-evidence classes · the five boundary regimes · the six Wilson stages · the fourfold Indian scheme |
| **B — only with source and date attached** | Published measurements and dated events | Caledonian ~320 Ma, Hercynian ~240 Ma, Alpine ~30 Ma · Archaean ~4,000 Ma · Dharwar > 3,100 / ~2,300 / ~1,000 Ma · Purana 1400-600 Ma · Lower Vindhyan 1,300-1,100 Ma, Upper 1,000-600 Ma · Dravidian period dates · Gondwana ~250 Ma and ~6,000 m · Deccan ~10 lakh → ~5 lakh sq km and its thickness set · Shiwalik 14-0.2 Ma and ~5,000 m · the three Himalayan upheaval phases · the 98 per cent coal proportion · coastline ~6,100 km as a book-era structural statement · every peak height in L14 |
| **C — never from memory** | Values that are revised, contested or environment-specific | ❗ Spreading rates · plate counts beyond seven major · collision dates outside a cited attribution · current production or reserve figures · crustal thickness under the Peninsula or the Himalaya · the number of physiographic sub-divisions · any Triassic thickness from the inconsistent passage · India's current coastline measurement, which belongs to another owner |

⚠️ **Four pre-writing checks**, in order, before a structural answer is begun: **directive** — what is actually being asked; **marks budget** — what a new band must buy; **number class** — A, B or C for every figure intended; **boundary** — whose topic owns each paragraph.

### Instrument 6 — Cross-topic deployment without ownership leakage

🔁 This topic's material is needed in several other answers, and the discipline is to **supply and route** rather than to annex.

| Where this topic is needed | What it supplies | What it must not do |
|---|---|---|
| Mineral and energy resources | The lithological substrate: shield and Dharwar for metals, Gondwana for coal, Tertiary sedimentary basins for petroleum | Develop reserves, production, auctions, mining economics or policy |
| Weathering, soils and groundwater | The parent material and its permeability behaviour | Teach regur or laterite as soils, or aquifer management |
| Vulcanism and earthquakes | The fissure flow as an **emplacement mechanism**, and seismic waves as **evidence** | Teach eruption styles, volcanic landforms, seismic zonation or hazard governance |
| Drainage and fluvial landforms | The structural cause — a dome, an escarpment, a rift — behind a drainage pattern | Teach the pattern itself |
| Glacial and arid landforms | The Indian ice-age record as **stratigraphic evidence** | Teach moraine morphology or periglacial features |
| The Earth and the universe | Nothing; it **receives** differentiation and the geodynamo from there | Re-derive differentiation or restate the coastline-length convention |

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | Memorising the finished map | Building the map in order |
|---|---|---|
| What is stored | A picture with sixteen labels | A **sequence** of seven depositional and igneous episodes |
| What it supports | Recognition | **Reconstruction**, and therefore explanation |
| Failure mode | A forgotten patch is unrecoverable | A forgotten patch can be re-derived from its position in the sequence |
| Transfer | None; the map is the map | The chronology transfers to every Indian structural question |

**Strongest objection.** Answer craft is padding. The marks are in the content, and a lesson spent on technique is a lesson not spent on rock.

**Reply.** Not in this topic, for a specific reason: **this topic's content is unusually easy to misallocate.** Its lithology touches resource questions it does not own, its seismic material touches hazard questions it does not own, and its numbers are a minefield in which one source contradicts another on the Alpine orogeny, one prints an impossible date for the term "Archaean", one misplaces Singareni and one gives two different Triassic thicknesses in a single sentence. ⚠️ A candidate with perfect content and no routing discipline writes resource geography in a structure question; a candidate with perfect content and no number protocol quotes the Deccan's present extent as its eruptive extent. **Both lose marks that no additional fact would have recovered.**

⚠️ **Residual.** Directive conventions are not perfectly stable between examiners and years; *describe*, *discuss* and *examine* shade into one another in practice. Treat the decoding table as a strong prior, read the actual wording, and let the question override the table where the two differ.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** serves both directly owned clauses — "Salient features of world's physical geography" (Mains GS-I) and "Indian and World Geography — Physical" (Prelims).
- **Verified linkage:** all seven owned demands, as routed instruments rather than as content. ❗ **Answer-neutral throughout**; no key is recorded or inferred for any objective demand.
- **Probable framing:** this lesson is never itself the question. It is what converts the preceding fourteen into marks.
- **Traps:** memorising the composite map instead of building it; conflating *tectonic* with *geomorphic* in a two-part objective demand; lengthening a ten-mark answer to fill a fifteen-mark space; quoting a Class C number; annexing a routed demand.
- **Answer use:** run the four pre-writing checks in order. They take under thirty seconds and they prevent the three errors — wrong demand, wrong length, wrong owner — that cost more than any missing fact.

Revision notes:

- **Build order for the composite geological map:** outline → **shield, granites, charnockites** → **Dharwar belts** → **Purana (Cuddapah crescent, Vindhyan spread)** → **Gondwana four belts** → **Deccan and Rajmahal flows** → **Tertiary including Shiwaliks and Tipam** → **Quaternary** → **lineaments last**.
- ❗ **The order is the content.** Drawing the build teaches the chronology; memorising the finished map does not.
- **Composite legend (sixteen classes):** Quaternary · Shiwaliks and Tipam · Lower and Middle Miocene · Palaeocene · Coastal Tertiary · Rajmahal and Deccan Flows · Mesozoic · Mesozoic and Palaeozoic windows · Gondwana and Upper Palaeozoic · Vindhyan and Cuddapah · Purana and Lower Palaeozoic · **Malani Volcanics** · Dharwar · Granites of all ages · Unclassified crystallines · **Charnockites**.
- **Nine assets:** M1 world structural divisions · M2 plates · M3 India composite · M4 eight single-system overlays · M5 four-panel palaeogeographic sequence · M6 Archaean with mineral legend · M7 Deccan thickness profile · M8 hill-range inventory · M9 lineaments (**Great Boundary Fault**, **Main Central Thrust**).
- ❗ Individual names from the series-and-faults list are **not quoted as decisive**; the two corroborated lineaments are the GBF and the MCT.
- **Directive decoding:** *characteristics and types* → six criteria + **two axes** + **forms** · *changes in continents and ocean basins* → mechanism **plus** the life cycle with living examples · *evidences of drift* → five classes **with their logic** · *formation of fold mountains* → convergence → shortening → thickening → isostatic uplift · *rock type and human activity* → petrology → weathering → soil/aquifer/mineral → land use · *isostasy* → **Airy against Pratt** plus observable adjustment.
- **Tectonic ≠ geomorphic.** Tectonic = age, stability, deformation, marine history. Geomorphic = relief, surface form, structural style.
- **Evidence ladder:** **10** = thesis + 3-4 steps + Indian example + qualification · **15** = + a second mode of evidence **and** an evaluation paragraph · **20** = + a second axis and a **graded verdict**. Extra marks buy a **new kind** of content.
- **Four evidence units:** ocean-basin life cycle · tectonic setting fixes hydrocarbons and earthquakes · rock genesis governs surface geography · **India's mineral and agricultural geographies do not coincide, and the reason is lithological** — each with claim, evidence, significance and limitation.
- **Number protocol: Class A** quote freely · **Class B** source and date attached · ❗ **Class C never from memory** — spreading rates, plate counts beyond seven major, unattributed collision dates, current production or reserves, Indian crustal thicknesses, sub-division counts, Triassic thicknesses, the current coastline measurement.
- **Four pre-writing checks:** **directive · marks budget · number class · boundary.**
- 🔁 **Supply and route:** minerals and energy · soils and groundwater · vulcanism and earthquakes · drainage · glacial landforms · and the planetary topic, from which this one *receives* rather than supplies.

### Concept check

**Question:** Why does this edition insist that the composite geological map of India be drawn as a build rather than memorised as a finished object, and what specific examination advantage does the build order confer?

**Model answer:** Because the map's legend is a chronology in disguise, and drawing it in order forces the chronology to be learnt as a sequence of events rather than as sixteen labels. The build runs shields, granites and charnockites first, because everything rests on them; then the Dharwar belts within the shield; then the Purana basins, with the Cuddapah crescent and the Vindhyan spread; then the four Gondwana belts; then the Deccan and Rajmahal flows; then the Tertiary including the Shiwaliks along the Himalayan front; then the Quaternary; and lineaments last. The advantage is recoverability. A candidate who has memorised a picture and forgets a patch has no way back to it, whereas a candidate who has learnt the build can re-derive any missing unit from its position in the sequence, because each layer is defined by what it lies upon and what lies upon it. The order also encodes the unconformities, which a finished map hides.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat mapwork as a presentational skill to be added after the content is learnt. In this topic the map order *is* a content structure; drawing it in the wrong order produces a map that is spatially correct and chronologically meaningless.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "In questions on India's geological structure, the commonest loss of marks is not ignorance but misallocation." Examine, with reference to directive decoding and ownership boundaries. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The claim holds for three reasons. First, directives differ in what they license: *describe* wants ordered content on the right axes, *discuss* wants a position developed and qualified, and *comment on isostasy* wants Airy against Pratt rather than a plate-tectonics summary — so a well-informed script can answer a question it was not asked. Second, mark bands buy different kinds of content, not more of the same: a fifteen-mark tectonics answer earns its upper band from the ocean-basin life cycle with living examples and from a consequence paragraph, so a longer ten-mark answer remains a ten-mark answer. Third, this topic's lithology touches demands it does not own, since Deccan resource potential and Gondwanaland mining economics belong to the resource owner and basalt carbonation to the environment owner; naming the substrate and routing is correct, while developing it spends marks elsewhere. The qualification is that misallocation and ignorance often co-occur.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for directive decoding illustrated with at least two contrasting directives; 3 for the marks ladder explained as a change in *kind* of content; 2 for at least two correctly routed adjacent demands with their owners named; 1 for the number-class protocol mentioned; 1 for the qualification. Deduct 2 for an answer that treats craft as presentation rather than allocation.

# VERIFIED PYQ LINKAGE AND ANSWER APPROACHES

## What this topic owns, and what it does not

This topic directly owns **seven verified demands**. ❗ **Both Mains demands lie in the world half** — primary rocks and crustal tectonics, worth **twenty-five marks between them** — and the **India half owns no Mains demand at all**. The remaining five are objective, and two of those five are India-half demands.

❗ **This topic does own a 2026 demand**, which is a direct contrast with the adjacent Topic 01 lane. The 2026 General Studies Paper I ledger carries twenty routed demands and **none reaches this topic**; the 2026 Essay ledger, the 2026 GS-II and GS-III ledgers, the three Civil Services Aptitude Test ledgers and both GS-III/GS-IV ledgers likewise carry **no route here**. The single 2026 route is a **Prelims** route, on the Peninsular Block.

❗ **No answer letter is recorded or inferred for any objective question below.** That includes the two 2024-25 questions whose official Set-A keys are physically present in the repository, and it includes the 2026 question whose Set-A key is held locally but is **provisional**. No truth marking, elimination cue or corrective mapping appears anywhere in this file. ❗ **No solved previous-year model answer appears**, and no replacement solved-practice workbook has been created.

## Approach notes — demand by demand

**P1 · 2022 GS-I Q4 · Describe · 10 marks · 150 words.** *Describe* wants ordered content, not evaluation. Define **primary** in the material sense — igneous rock is the chief original source of the crust's inorganic mineral matter — in a single clause, and do not argue it chronologically. Give the characteristics through the six-criterion frame rather than as loose adjectives: texture, structure, colour, permeability, mode of occurrence, resistance to denudation. Give the types on **two axes**, acid against basic and plutonic against volcanic, producing the four-box grid. Then give the **forms**, which is where most scripts stop short: batholith at 100 sq km or more with no observable bottom, stock below that threshold, laccolith doming the strata, sill concordant, dike discordant, phacolith in a fold crest or trough; and on the extrusive side the explosive products against the quiet fissure flow. Close with one Indian case on each side — Deccan basalt and Closepet granite. ❗ Do not spend lines on the rock cycle; it was not asked.

**P2 · 2025 GS-I Q16 · Discuss · 15 marks · 250 words.** *Discuss* at fifteen marks wants a position developed through evidence and qualified at the end. Open on the thesis that continents and ocean basins are **transient configurations**, not permanent containers. Supply the evidence that the map changes — the five drift classes, one line each, with the shelf-edge refinement. Supply the mechanism — spreading with its four ocean-floor proofs and, decisively, the **creation-destruction balance**. Supply the structured expression — the boundary regimes and what each builds, keeping the three convergent sub-types apart. Then the paragraph that separates the script: the **six-stage life cycle with its living examples**, East African Rift through the Himalayan suture, with India occupying two stages at once. Add the human-geography consequence: passive margins and offshore hydrocarbons, active margins and hazard belts. Qualify twice — rates are slow and non-uniform, and the seven-plate scheme is a convention. Close by converting the whole into the observation that hazard zones, resource belts and coastline stability are **spatially organised rather than random**.

**P3 · 2018 Prelims Q57.** Mechanism owed: a cooling lava **locks in the polarity of the field at the moment it solidifies**, so the rock record preserves a sequence of **reversals**, which the ocean floor displays as symmetrical stripes about a spreading axis. 🔁 The field itself, the geodynamo and the shielding of the atmosphere are the planetary topic's property, and the consequences for atmospheric evolution and for life sit at that boundary. ❗ Name the recording mechanism, name the owner of the field, and stop. *Answer-neutral.*

**P4 · 2023 Prelims Q9.** Mechanism owed: the **structural typing of Indian hill systems**, each by mechanism rather than by height — the Aravali as a relict of folding at the close of the Archaean, the Satpura as a **horst**, the Western Ghats as a faulted escarpment breached at the Palghat Gap, and the Eastern Ghats as a **chain of highly broken and detached hills with neither structural unity nor physiographic continuity**. ⚠️ Note that **Amarkantak** occurs twice in this topic, as a Satpura summit at 1,127 m and as the eastern limit of the Deccan Trap at 150 m thickness. *Answer-neutral.*

**P5 · 2024 Prelims Q10.** Mechanism owed: the **four-class mountain typology with its mechanism and its orogenic date**. Fold mountains arise from compression and are *mountains of elevation*; block mountains such as the **Hunsruck, Vosges and Black Forest** are attributed to **tension**, with steep faulted edges and an almost level summit. On the orogenic rail, the **Appalachians** fall among the Hercynian ranges at about 240 Ma, and the **Alps** and the **Andes** among the Alpine ranges. ❗ A dating conflict between sources on the Alpine orogeny is disclosed at Lesson 5 and no single date is asserted. *Answer-neutral.*

**P6 · 2025 Prelims Q25.** Mechanism owed: the **five evidence classes with their inferential logic** — jig-saw fit, better at the continental-shelf edge than at the shoreline because coasts are erosional; geological continuity of belts and old rock provinces on reassembly; identical terrestrial floras and faunas on separated southern continents; palaeoclimatic anomalies, including glacial deposits in present tropics; and palaeomagnetism, whose force is **comparative**, since two continents with different apparent polar wander paths cannot both describe a single fixed pole. ⚠️ The accompanying analytic point is that the hypothesis was rejected **for want of a mechanism, not because the evidence was false**. *Answer-neutral.*

**P7 · 2026 Prelims Q34.** Mechanism owed, in the two separated lists the demand's wording requires. **Tectonic:** among the oldest land-masses; a stabilised shield; **little affected by tectonic forces**; ❗ **no marine submergence since the Cambrian period**, a bounded claim whose qualifier is the fact; and the corroborating absence of Dravidian rocks from the Peninsula except small patches near Umaria. **Geomorphic:** **open senile topography**; block-faulted uplands, escarpments and rift valleys rather than folded chains; a dissected residual plateau surface; and the included Meghalaya plateau and Kuchchh-Kathiawar region. ⚠️ The contrast term is an extra-Peninsula that is Tethyan in origin, carries the most youthful relief on earth, is earthquake-prone and is **folded, faulted and overthrust**. *Answer-neutral; the local 2026 key is provisional and is not consulted.*

⚠️ Three of these — the 2022 Deccan-resource demand, the 2021 Gondwanaland-mining demand and the 2023 basalt-carbonation demand — are genuinely **tempting**, because their lithology is this topic's lithology. ❗ The correct move is to **name the substrate, state the route and stop**, and Lesson 14 teaches that restraint as a skill rather than treating it as a limitation.

# CUMULATIVE CONCEPT CHECKS

Five checks. Three consolidate a teaching block, one tests the chronology thread that runs across the whole India half, and the fifth spans the topic — justified because the chain from a classification frame to an Indian resource geography cannot be tested inside any single block.

### Cumulative check A — the classification foundation, Lessons 1 to 4

**Question:** Build the chain from "the crust is an aggregate of minerals" to "an unconformity records five events", naming every intermediate step, and identify the point at which description becomes chronology.

**Model answer:** One: a rock is an aggregate of minerals, so its behaviour under weathering is a weighted average of its constituents and its name denotes an assemblage rather than a substance. Two: rocks differ on six criteria — texture, structure, colour, permeability, mode of occurrence and resistance to denudation — which is the frame any characteristics question is marked against. Three: on the criterion of genesis they fall into three families; igneous from cooled magma, sedimentary from accumulated sediment and organic matter, metamorphic from pre-existing rock altered by heat, pressure and fluids. Four: texture encodes cooling rate, so coarse means plutonic and fine means volcanic, and that is a genetic statement disguised as a descriptive one. Five: sedimentary rock is **stratified**, and stratification permits superposition, so a stack becomes a sequence. Six: igneous rock is non-fossiliferous while sedimentary rock may carry fossils, so correlation between basins becomes possible where fossils exist and hard where they do not. Seven: a surface truncating folded beds beneath horizontal beds above records deposition, deformation, uplift, erosion and renewed deposition — five events with no rock to represent the interval. Description becomes chronology at step five, where stratification stops being a diagnostic property and becomes a clock.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat the chronology toolkit as an add-on to the classification. Superposition, fossils, unconformities and cross-cutting relations are all consequences of the family properties taught for identification; the same facts do both jobs.

### Cumulative check B — the tectonic argument, Lessons 5 to 8

**Question:** Three dates — 1914, 1960 and 1967 — mark the history of one idea. State what each secured, what was still missing after each, and why the orogenic chronology of Lesson 5 remains necessary even after 1967.

**Model answer:** **1914** secured the *claim and its evidential base*: Wegener's comprehensive case for a single Pangaea surrounded by Panthalassa, supported by the jig-saw fit, geological continuity, fossil distribution and palaeoclimatic anomaly. What was missing was a **mechanism**; his centrifugal pole-fleeing force and the tidal pull of Moon and Sun were physically inadequate, and the hypothesis was rejected on that ground rather than on evidential grounds. **1960** secured the *mechanism*: Hess's sea-floor spreading on asthenospheric convection, which made the ocean floor an active element and generated four testable predictions — symmetrical magnetic stripes, age increasing from the axis, sediment thickness increasing with age, and trenches consuming old floor. What was still missing was a **general kinematic framework**: spreading describes ocean floor, not the motion of rigid blocks carrying continents and floor together. **1967** secured that framework, and drift was absorbed rather than merely confirmed. The orogenic chronology remains necessary because plate tectonics supplies the **mechanism** and not the **periodisation**: "convergent margin" is equally true of the Caledonian, the Hercynian and the Alpine belts and therefore distinguishes none of them, whereas the dated classes predict present height, sharpness, seismicity and degree of denudation together.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat 1967 as the moment the evidence improved. The drift evidence did not change between 1914 and 1967; the model within which it was interpreted did, which is why the episode is a lesson about mechanism rather than about data.

### Cumulative check C — the bridge, Lessons 8, 9 and 13

**Question:** Show how a single tectonic event produced all three of India's structural provinces, and state precisely where in that sequence each province acquires its defining character.

**Model answer:** The event is the fissuring of Gondwanaland and the northward transit of the Indian block into Eurasia. The **Peninsular block** acquires its character *before* the event and is defined by what did not happen to it: a stabilised shield of crystalline and metamorphic rock, little affected by tectonic forces, with an open senile topography produced by prolonged denudation, and with no marine submergence since the Cambrian — the Dravidian gap being the corroborating absence. The **extra-Peninsula** acquires its character *during* the event: the Tethys, a geosyncline that in the Upper Carboniferous linked the Pacific to the Atlantic, is consumed in a continent-continent collision, so the resulting belt is folded, faulted and overthrust, carries the most youthful relief on earth, is seismically very active and volcanically quiet, and rose in three phases — Eocene producing the Greater Himalayas, mid-Miocene the Lesser, post-Pliocene the Shiwaliks. The **Indo-Gangetic plain** acquires its character *as a consequence*: the load of the rising range downwarped its foreland into a foredeep of variable depth, formed concomitantly with the uplift, which the range's own erosion products then filled — which is why the plain had not figured at all until the Quaternary. One event, three provinces, three different relations to it.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not place the plain's origin alongside the other two as a parallel third fact. It is downstream of both, and dating it with them destroys the causal order that makes the three-province scheme an explanation rather than a list.

### Cumulative check D — the Indian chronology thread, Lessons 10 to 13

**Question:** Take the fourfold Indian scheme and, for each rung, state one piece of evidence that it is **a scheme** rather than a reading the rocks impose, then say why the scheme is nevertheless retained.

**Model answer:** **Archaean and Dharwar:** Wadia holds the Dharwars to be the most ancient metamorphosed sedimentary system of India, as old as or in some cases older than the basement gneisses and schists, so the ordering of the two lowest rungs is a convention rather than a demonstrated superposition. **Purana:** a second respected source places the Cuddapah and Vindhyan sequences, with the Aravalli and the Dharwar, inside an "Archaean System" and uses "Purana" for the Pre-Cambrian era instead, so the very existence of this rung is contested. **Dravidian:** the rung is defined largely by an absence, since Dravidian rocks are conspicuous by their absence in the Peninsula apart from small patches near Umaria, which means its content depends on which region is being classified. **Aryan:** the rung is held together by a warning rather than a shared character, since Gondwana must be kept out of the Dravidian, and it contains sediments, a flood basalt and a mountain-building episode in one bracket. The scheme is nevertheless retained because it makes the unconformities meaningful, it organises the mineral geography — metals low, fuels high — and it makes the Cambrian submergence claim statable at all. Naming the frame as a frame is what converts recitation into control.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not resolve the disagreement by mixing the two schemes. Taking occurrence and etymology from one source and chronology from the other produces a sequence that contradicts itself, which is worse than adopting either scheme consistently.

### Cumulative check E — the whole topic, Lessons 1 to 15

**Question:** In one connected argument, state what this topic establishes about the relationship between a rock and a human geography, drawing on at least five lessons, and say exactly where the relationship stops holding.

**Model answer:** The topic's recurring claim is that lithology sets the distribution of possibilities at each successive node, and that the relationship loosens as the chain lengthens. It begins tight. Composition fixes density and colour, so acid granitic material stands above basic material — the sial-over-sima contrast that makes continents exist (L1, L3). Genesis fixes structure and permeability, so a permeable limestone can stand as a steep-sided hill while a shale of comparable hardness is worn into low ground; the causal variable is permeability, not hardness (L2, L3). Orogenic age fixes relief and seismicity together, so a Hercynian belt is low and quiet while an Alpine belt is high and active (L5). Tectonic position fixes resource and hazard distribution, since passive margins carry sediment wedges and offshore hydrocarbons while active margins carry trenches and arcs (L8). In India the chain yields its cleanest controlled comparison: the alluvial plain is extremely useful for agriculture and almost completely devoid of minerals, while the crystalline Peninsula, especially Chotanagpur, is the reverse, and the variable that differs is the rock (L14). The relationship stops holding beyond the node of **possibility**. Geological association supports a probability, not a prediction; soil also depends on climate, relief, organisms and time; and extraction depends on grade, depth, infrastructure, price and policy. Structure explains the pattern of opportunity, not the outcome.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not read this as licence for determinism in its weak form either. The fan does not become determinism merely because its early nodes are tight; the honest claim is that each node narrows a distribution, and the number of nodes between rock and livelihood is large.

# ORIGINAL 10-, 15- AND 20-MARK MAINS MODEL PRACTICE

⚠️ These are **original practice prompts written for this edition**. They are not previous-year questions and must not be cited as such. None duplicates a lesson-local practice question. Every rubric is specific to its own answer.

## 10-mark practice (about 150 words)

**Practice question 1 [10] — mechanism and classification.** "Texture is a historical record, not merely a descriptive property." Explain, with reference to igneous rocks. *(Explain — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** Texture records the rate at which a melt lost heat, and the rate of heat loss is fixed by the depth at which solidification occurred. Slow cooling at depth allows crystals to grow large, so a coarse texture indicates a plutonic setting; rapid chilling at or above the surface allows only tiny crystals, so a fine texture indicates a volcanic one. Reading the property as a record lets one reconstruct a history that was not observed: a coarse-grained granite now exposed at the surface must also have undergone prolonged denudation to strip the cover under which it formed, as the Closepet granite of the Dharwar terrain did. Conversely the fine-grained Deccan basalt, erupted from fissures as extremely mobile lava, records chilling at the surface. The qualification is that the inference is general rather than exact: a single body may be coarse at its centre and fine at its chilled margin.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the inverse relation between crystal size and cooling rate, with depth named as the controlling variable; 2 for texture used to reconstruct an unobserved history; 2 for one Indian plutonic and one Indian volcanic case; 2 for the denudation inference attached to an exposed pluton; 1 for the chilled-margin qualification. Deduct 2 for assigning basalt a coarse texture.

**Practice question 2 [10] — evidence and its limits.** Examine the claim that the near-total absence of one rock system from Peninsular India is among the strongest pieces of evidence about that region's history. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The system is the Dravidian, and the claim holds. Dravidian rocks are conspicuous by their absence in Peninsular India except for one or two small patches of lower Permian age near Umaria, and fossil-bearing Cambrian and Devonian rocks are unknown there, while the system is comparatively well developed in the extra-Peninsula, which was Tethyan sea through the Palaeozoic. The absence is therefore the predicted observation of the claim that the Peninsula has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian period: a block standing above sea level accumulates no marine sediment. The evidence is strong because it is a prediction that could have failed and did not. Two limits apply. An absence can mean non-deposition or later removal, and the inference here depends on the independent evidence of a stable, long-emergent, senile block. And the claim is bounded by its qualifier, since pre-Cambrian marine sequences in the Peninsula are abundant.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the Dravidian absence stated precisely, including the Umaria exception; 3 for the absence used as a confirmed prediction of the submergence claim; 2 for the non-deposition against removal limitation; 1 for the extra-Peninsular contrast; 1 for the Cambrian qualifier handled correctly. Deduct 2 for an unqualified "never submerged".

## 15-mark practice (about 250 words)

**Practice question 3 [15] — chronology and causation.** "In India, the Tertiary did not merely add rocks; it reorganised the country." Discuss. *(Discuss — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The claim is well founded. The Tertiary is described as the most significant period in India's geological history because the Himalayas were born and India's present form came into being within it. Three reorganisations follow. First, a mountain system was created where an ocean had been: the Tethys geosyncline, which in the Upper Carboniferous linked the Pacific eastward through China and the Atlantic westward through Afghanistan, Iran, Asia Minor and the present Mediterranean, was consumed in collision, and the belt rose in three phases — Eocene at about 65 million years producing the Greater Himalayas, mid-Miocene at about 45 million years folding the Lesser Himalayas, and post-Pliocene at about 1.4 million years folding the Shiwaliks, which are the range's own debris caught up by a southward-advancing front. Second, a new structural province was created: the load of the rising range downwarped its foreland into a foredeep of variable depth, formed concomitantly with the uplift, so the Great Plain is a tectonic object before it is a depositional one and had not figured at all until the Quaternary. Third, the country's outline was fixed, since the Arabian Sea and the Bay of Bengal came into being in the Cretaceous or early Tertiary after Gondwanaland disintegrated, and India thereafter assumed its present shape. The reorganisation is also economic, since the Shiwalik Dupi Tila beds contain the Lakhimpur and Digboi oil fields and Tertiary coal is important in the north-east. Two qualifications: the dates are the cited authors', and the Himalayas still rise.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** 3 for the pivot claim stated and justified; 3 for the Tethys-to-Himalaya conversion with the geosyncline's linkages; 3 for the three dated upheaval phases attributed to their source; 3 for the foredeep as a tectonic creation, with the Quaternary timing — an answer that treats the plain as mere deposition cannot exceed 9; 2 for the outline fixation and the two new seas; 1 for the attribution and still-rising qualifications. Deduct 2 for any volcanic Himalaya and 2 for an unattributed collision date.

**Practice question 4 [15] — comparison across the two halves.** Compare the Deccan Trap and the Columbia-Snake province as expressions of one process, and assess what the comparison adds to an understanding of India's geological structure. *(Compare and assess — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** Both are volcanic plateaux in the standard typology, built by the same emplacement mechanism rather than by cone-building. Basic lava is iron- and magnesium-rich, denser and darker than acid lava and, decisively, far more mobile, so when it issues from fissures and cracks rather than from a vent it spreads laterally as sheets. In the Deccan, extremely mobile basalt flooded about ten lakh sq km between the close of the Cretaceous and the early Eocene, covering the pre-existing topography entirely; successive flows with flat tops and steep fronts give the stepped profile from which the name *trap*, the Swedish word for stair, derives. The comparison adds three things to an Indian reading. It establishes that the Deccan's form is a product of **lava rheology**, not of Indian peculiarity, which is why the same typology lists Antrim and the Columbia-Snake alongside it. It isolates what *is* peculiar to India: the scale of subsequent loss, since denudation has halved the province to about five lakh sq km, so the Deccan is simultaneously a volcanic plateau by origin and a dissected residual upland by present form. And it clarifies the inter-trappean beds as evidence of episodic rather than continuous eruption. The assessment must concede a limit: this edition holds no comparative data on the Columbia-Snake's extent, thickness or age, so the comparison is one of process and form rather than of magnitude, and no numerical ranking between the two provinces is asserted here.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** 3 for fissure eruption of mobile basic lava identified as the shared mechanism; 3 for the Deccan's extent, date bracket and the denudation correction; 3 for the dual classification as volcanic plateau and residual upland; 2 for inter-trappean beds read as evidence of episodicity; 2 for the etymology and the stepped profile explained; 2 for the explicit refusal to assert comparative magnitudes without data. Deduct 3 for quoting the present extent as the eruption's extent.

## 20-mark practice (about 250 words)

**Practice question 5 [20] — the full causal fan with its counter-case.** "Geological structure explains the map of India's opportunities, not the map of its outcomes." Critically examine. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The first half of the statement is strongly supported. Structure fans out into relief, drainage, groundwater, soil parent material and mineral association, and from those into land use, irrigation potential, transport alignment and settlement. The controlled case is decisive: the alluvial Indo-Gangetic plain is extremely useful for agriculture yet almost completely devoid of mineral deposits, while the igneous and metamorphic Peninsular plateau, especially Chotanagpur, is very rich — two adjacent regions, opposite endowments, one differing variable. The mechanism is intelligible at each node: Archaean and Dharwar crystallines host metallic minerals, Gondwana troughs hold the overwhelming share of India's coal, Tertiary sedimentary basins hold petroleum, and crystalline terrain stores water in fractures rather than pores. The second half is equally supported, and the counter-case proves it. Lithology fixes where ores *can* occur; grade, depth, accessibility, infrastructure, price and policy fix what is extracted, which is why resource potential and mining output are separate questions owned by a different part of the syllabus. Soil formation depends on climate, relief, organisms and time as well as on parent rock, so basalt *produces* regur without determining an agricultural economy. And the plain's own wealth shows that mineral absence is not economic absence. A critical examination must also concede that the evidence is itself conventional: the six-division scheme is one of several, and peak heights, plateau areas and sub-division counts should never be quoted from memory. Structure narrows a distribution at every node; the number of nodes between rock and livelihood is large.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** 4 for the fan set out with at least four parallel branches; 4 for the controlled Gangetic-against-Chotanagpur comparison used as evidence; 4 for lithology-specific resource associations across at least three systems; 4 for the counter-case — association not determination, multiple causation, and the routing of extraction economics to its owner; 2 for the conventionality of the division scheme conceded; 2 for a graded verdict that distinguishes opportunity from outcome. Deduct 3 for a deterministic conclusion and 2 for any memory-quoted height or area.

**Practice question 6 [20] — method and evidence across the whole topic.** "In the study of the Earth's crust, the strongest conclusions rest on observations that could have come out otherwise." Critically examine, drawing on both the world and the Indian halves of the subject. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original):** The proposition identifies what distinguishes a strong geological conclusion from a plausible one. Sea-floor spreading is the clearest world case. Proposed by Hess in 1960 on asthenospheric convection, it predicted that crust created at a ridge axis and carried outward must record the field's polarity at its moment of cooling, producing stripes **mirrored** about the axis; that age must increase away from the axis; that sediment thickness must follow age; and that old floor must be destroyed at trenches or the Earth would expand. Each prediction could have failed; none did, and the symmetry in particular is reproduced by no competing model. Continental drift is the contrasting case: its evidence was distributional and consistent, but it generated no risky prediction and offered no adequate mechanism, which is why it was rejected for more than fifty years rather than refuted. The Indian half supplies a third form. The claim that the Peninsula has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian predicts an absence of Palaeozoic marine sediment, and the Dravidian system is indeed conspicuously absent apart from small patches near Umaria. Critical examination must qualify in two directions. An absence is weaker than a presence, since non-deposition and later removal are both compatible with it. And several of this subject's numbers are not risky observations at all but conventions or contested measurements — the Alpine orogeny is dated irreconcilably between sources, so the ordinal claim travels and the figure does not.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** 4 for risky prediction identified as the criterion and applied, not merely asserted; 4 for the four spreading predictions with symmetry singled out; 4 for drift treated as the contrasting case, rejected for want of mechanism rather than refuted; 4 for the Dravidian absence used as an Indian instance of a confirmed prediction; 2 for the asymmetry between absence and presence conceded; 2 for the distinction between risky observations and conventions, with the Alpine dating conflict named. Deduct 3 for claiming that drift's evidence was disproved.

# REMEDIATION

Eighteen errors that recur when this topic is used in an answer, each with the reason it fails, the repair and the lesson that prevents it.

| # | Error | Why it fails | Repair | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Calling the Deccan Trap a sedimentary formation | The stepped profile imitates bedding but is produced by successive lava flows; the rock is extrusive flood basalt | "The Deccan Trap is igneous — fissure-fed flood basalt; the 'steps' are successive flows, hence *trap*, from the Swedish for stair" | **L12** |
| R2 | Placing India's coal in the Deccan rather than the Gondwana | Coal is a carbonaceous sedimentary rock and cannot occur as a constituent of a basalt province | "Gondwana rocks contain about 98 per cent of India's coal reserves; Tertiary coal is important in the north-east" | **L12** |
| R3 | Describing the Himalaya as volcanic in origin | A continent-continent collision belt is seismically very active but volcanically quiet, because no slab is descending to generate arc magma | "The Himalaya is a collision orogen — seismically active, volcanically quiet; the Andes is a subduction orogen" | **L13**, premise at L8 |
| R4 | Collapsing Dharwar into "all Archaean rocks" | The Dharwar is a distinct metamorphosed supracrustal sequence with its own cycles, dates and mineral association | "Archaean basement gneiss-granite complex; Dharwar a separate metamorphosed sequence — and Wadia held it as old as or older" | **L10** |
| R5 | Placing the Gondwana system inside the Dravidian | The Dravidian is substantially absent from the Peninsula; the Gondwana is the Peninsula's defining Aryan sequence | "Gondwana is Aryan. The Dravidian is conspicuous by its absence in the Peninsula apart from small patches near Umaria" | **L11, L12** |
| R6 | Quoting the present ~5 lakh sq km Deccan extent as the eruption's extent | It understates the event by half and conceals that the province is a denudation residue | "About ten lakh sq km erupted; denudation has since halved it to about five lakh sq km" | **L12** |
| R7 | Treating *bhangar* and *khadar* as soil types | They are alluvial stratigraphic units defined by age and position, with soil consequences owned elsewhere | "*Bhangar* = older alluvium, Middle-to-Upper Pleistocene; *khadar* = newer alluvium, Upper Pleistocene, flood-prone lower valleys" | **L13** |
| R8 | "The Peninsula has never been submerged", without the Cambrian qualifier | Pre-Cambrian marine sequences in the Peninsula are abundant; the unqualified claim is false | "The Indian Peninsula has not undergone marine submergence **since the Cambrian period**" | **L9** |
| R9 | Describing the Eastern Ghats as a continuous range | They are a chain of highly broken and detached hills with neither structural unity nor physiographic continuity, repeatedly breached by rivers | "The name is a misnomer; Spate preferred 'Eastern Hills', and the system divides into Eastern Ghats, Cuddapah Ranges and Tamilnad Hills" | **L14** |
| R10 | Treating the Aravali as a currently high mountain | Its low elevation is the evidence for its antiquity, not an anomaly to be explained away | "The Aravali is the relict of the world's oldest mountain, folded at the close of the Archaean; Chatterjee assigns it an Algonkian age of 600-700 Ma" | **L5, L14** |
| R11 | "Wegener's drift was rejected because the evidence was wrong" | The evidence was assembled convincingly; the two proposed driving forces were physically inadequate | "It was rejected for want of a viable mechanism, not because the evidence was false" | **L6** |
| R12 | Treating sea-floor spreading and plate tectonics as the same theory | Spreading was proposed in 1960 and concerns the creation and destruction of ocean floor; plate tectonics was established in 1967 and concerns rigid lithospheric plates | "Spreading (1960) supplied the mechanism; plate tectonics (1967) supplied the kinematic framework and absorbed drift" | **L7, L8** |
| R13 | Asserting a spreading rate, a plate count beyond seven major plates, or a collision date without a source | These are revised, convention-dependent or source-specific, and a wrong one is identifiable with certainty | "Seven major plates and several minor ones, the exact count being a convention"; quote a date only with its source attached | **L1, L8** |
| R14 | Attributing landscape resistance to hardness alone, ignoring permeability | A permeable limestone stands as a steep-sided hill while a shale of comparable hardness is worn into low ground | "The causal variable is permeability and mineral stability, not hardness alone" | **L3, L14** |
| R15 | Classifying the Vosges as a fold mountain | Large-scale block mountains and rift valleys are attributed to tension, not compression; the profile is scarp-bounded with an almost level summit | "The Vosges, Hunsruck and Black Forest are block mountains produced by tension as the surrounding land subsided" | **L5** |
| R16 | Quoting book-era production or reserve figures as current | The statistical appendices of the source volumes derive from mid-2000s compilations and several administrative names have since changed | Exclude the tables; quote the **structural proportion** with its attribution and use current administrative names | **L14**, applied at L12 |
| R17 | Reading the structure-to-resource chain as determinism rather than association | Geological association supports a probability; climate, time, technology, price and policy act at every node | "Structure sets the pattern of opportunity; extraction and land use are decided by grade, depth, infrastructure, price and policy" | **L14** |
| R18 | Describing the Indo-Gangetic plain as merely a depositional surface | It conceals the foredeep, and with it the plain's depth of fill, its Quaternary timing and its position | "The plain's floor is a synclinal basin — a downwarp of the Himalayan foreland formed concomitantly with the rise of the range" | **L13**, premise at L9 |

## Map 2 — The theory ladder, 1914 to 1967

| | **Drift, 1914** | **Spreading, 1960** | **Plate tectonics, 1967** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Proposer | **Wegener** — meteorologist, polar explorer, astronomer, geologist | **H. Harry Hess**, Princeton | — |
| Central object | **Pangaea** and **Panthalassa**; Laurasia and Gondwana | The **ocean floor** | The **rigid lithospheric plate** |
| Evidence | Fit (at the **shelf edge**), geological continuity, fossils, palaeoclimate, palaeomagnetism | Symmetrical stripes, age from the axis, sediment thickness, trenches | All of the above plus boundary seismicity |
| Mechanism | ❗ **Inadequate** — pole-fleeing and tidal forces | **Asthenospheric convection** | Inherited and generalised |
| Fatal gap at the time | No mechanism | No general kinematics | — |
| Outcome | **Rejected, not refuted** | Testable, and tested | Drift **absorbed** |
