## Map 4 — The causal fan, with its stopping rule

```text
   STRUCTURE
      |--> RELIEF ............ plateau | scarp | rift | plain
      |--> DRAINAGE .......... pattern follows structure (-> fluvial owner)
      |--> GROUNDWATER ....... pore storage vs FRACTURE storage
      |--> SOIL PARENT ....... basalt -> regur; crystallines -> red
      |                        (-> soils owner at the word "produces")
      |--> MINERAL ASSOCIATION shield/Dharwar -> METALS
                               Gondwana -> COAL (~98%)
                               Tertiary basins -> PETROLEUM
                                 |
                                 v
   LAND USE | IRRIGATION | TRANSPORT ALIGNMENT | SETTLEMENT DENSITY |
   HAZARD UNDERSTANDING
                                 |
   ============== THE STOPPING RULE, APPLIED AT EVERY NODE ===========
   association, NOT determination | climate, relief, time and organisms
   act at the soil node | technology, price, infrastructure and policy
   act at the resource node | the divisions themselves are one scheme
   among several
   ==================================================================
   PROOF THAT THE FAN IS REAL: alluvial plain = agriculturally
   excellent, almost NO minerals || crystalline Peninsula, especially
   CHOTANAGPUR = mineral-rich. Adjacent regions, opposite endowments,
   one differing variable.
```

## Map 5 — Mechanism against periodisation: why both are needed

| Question a candidate faces | Plate tectonics answers | Orogenic chronology answers | Needed together because |
|---|---|---|---|
| Why do mountains form here? | Convergence at a plate boundary | — | Mechanism alone does not date |
| Why is this range high and that one low? | — | Alpine against Hercynian or Caledonian class | Periodisation alone does not explain |
| Why is this belt seismic and that one quiet? | Boundary regime and whether a slab descends | Age and whether deformation is active | The two answers must agree |
| Why is the Himalaya volcanically silent? | Continent-continent collision; no descending slab | Alpine, and still rising | Mechanism supplies the silence; chronology supplies the rise |
| Why is the Aravali low? | — | Folded at the close of the Archaean; the oldest orogeny | Only chronology answers this |
| Why is the Deccan a plateau and not a chain of cones? | Fissure eruption of mobile basic lava | End-Cretaceous to early Eocene, then halved by denudation | Mechanism gives the form; chronology gives the loss |

## Map 6 — The standing cautions, as one pre-writing checklist

```text
  BEFORE WRITING ANY STRUCTURAL ANSWER, RUN FOUR CHECKS
  =====================================================================
  1 DIRECTIVE  describe / discuss / explain / examine / comment --
               what does the word license, and what does it forbid?
  2 BUDGET     10 = thesis + 3-4 steps + example + qualification
               15 = + a SECOND MODE of evidence + an evaluation para
               20 = + a SECOND AXIS + a graded verdict
               >> extra marks buy a NEW KIND of content <<
  3 NUMBER     A quote freely | B source and date attached |
               C NEVER from memory: spreading rates, plate counts
               beyond seven major, unattributed collision dates,
               current production or reserves, Indian crustal
               thicknesses, sub-division counts, Triassic thicknesses,
               the current coastline measurement
  4 BOUNDARY   whose topic owns this paragraph? minerals and energy |
               soils and groundwater | vulcanism and earthquakes |
               drainage | glacial landforms | the planetary topic |
               environment | disaster management | economy |
               art and culture
  =====================================================================
  THE EIGHTEEN REMEDIATION ERRORS ALL FAIL ONE OF THESE FOUR CHECKS.
```

# COMPLETE CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

Compressed for retrieval. This section changes form and does not repeat the lessons' explanatory prose; where an exam-tight statement must recur, the explanation is stripped out so that recall density stays high. Fifteen headings, one per lesson.

## A. The Crust in Section

**Geomorphology** as a term arose in the **US Geological Survey in the 1880s**, possibly coined by **J.W. Powell and W.J. McGee**. **Crust** = compositional unit, ends at the **Moho**, average ~**35 km**. **Lithosphere** = mechanical unit, **75-125 km**, crust **plus** rigid uppermost mantle. ❗ **Plates are lithospheric, not crustal.** **Asthenosphere 250-700 km**, soft and mobile. **Sial** silica + alumina, density ~**2.7**, granitic; **sima** silica + iron + magnesium, ~**3.0**, basaltic — Leong, with crust **3-4 miles** oceanic to **30 miles** continental, mantle/**mesosphere** ~**1,800 miles** olivine-rich, core/**barysphere** radius ~**2,160 miles**, iron + nickel = **nife**, ~**3,500 °F**; ⚠️ imperial, mid-century, attribute. Metric corroboration: oceanic crust ~**5 km**, continental **30-40 km**. **Gutenberg** ~**2,900 km**; section markers **305 | 700 | 2,900 | 5,150 | 6,371 km**; crust **5-65 km**. **P** compressional, fastest, crosses solids and liquids; **S** shear, **cannot cross a liquid**; surface waves slowest and destructive (🔁 hazard owner). **Shadow zone** → liquid shell → **four inferences**: solid inner core, liquid outer core, soft asthenosphere, rigid lithosphere. Sharp **refraction** at 2,900 km → sharp boundary. ⚠️ Wave data fix **depth, state, sharpness** — **not composition**. 🇮🇳 Moho shallow under the Peninsula, deep under thickened Himalayan crust; ❗ **no number**. 🔁 Differentiation and geodynamo → planetary topic; eruption and seismic hazard → vulcanism owner.

## B. Three Families and One Cycle

**Six criteria:** **texture · structure · colour · permeability · mode of occurrence · resistance to denudation** — the scoring skeleton for any "characteristics and types" directive. **Rock = an aggregate of minerals**; rock-formers: **feldspars, micas, amphiboles, pyroxenes, olivine, quartz, calcite, dolomite, clays, gypsum**. **Igneous** from cooled magma: **plutonic/intrusive** slow at depth → **large crystals** (granite); **volcanic/extrusive** fast at surface → **tiny crystals** (basalt, lava sheets and plateaux); classified by **texture + composition** (basalt, gabbro, andesite, diorite, rhyolite, granite) **and** by mode of occurrence. "**Primary**" = **chief original source of inorganic mineral matter**, ❗ not a chronological claim. **Sedimentary** = accumulated sediment + organic matter, **stratified**, inches to many feet; agents stream, glacier, wind, organisms. **Metamorphic** = altered by **temperature, pressure, chemically active fluids**; **texture and composition** both change. Pairs: **shale/mudstone → slate or schist · limestone → marble · sandstone → quartzite · granite → gneiss**. ❌ **Foliation ≠ stratification** — foliation is metamorphic alignment; a schist splits along it. ⚠️ Coal → graphite is an example, ❗ never extended to diamond. **Secondary permeability** — joints, fractures, weathered mantle — governs water in crystalline rock. **The cycle is closed; no fixed start.** 🇮🇳 Exemplars: Deccan basalt · Closepet granite · Vindhyan sandstone · Gondwana coal · Bengal Gneiss/Khondolite · Charnockite.

## C. Primary Rocks in Depth

**Acid** — silica-rich, **less dense, lighter in colour** (granite, rhyolite). **Basic** — richer in **iron, aluminium, magnesium** oxides, **denser and darker** (basalt, gabbro). Same contrast at crustal scale = sial over sima. **Texture rule:** crystal size inversely related to cooling rate; cooling rate set by **depth of emplacement**. **Grid:** granite (acid plutonic) · gabbro (basic plutonic) · rhyolite (acid volcanic) · **basalt (basic volcanic)**. **Forms:** **batholith ≥ 100 sq km, no observable bottom** · **stock < 100 sq km** · **laccolith** domes the strata · **sill** concordant along bedding · **dike** discordant across bedding · **phacolith** in a fold crest or trough, **saddle reefs and gold-quartz veins**. Extrusive: explosive **bombs, lapilli, dust, ash** against quiet **fissure** sheets. Characteristics: crystalline · **unstratified** · **non-fossiliferous** · massive and jointed · low primary permeability · two classification axes · source of primary minerals. **Landscape control:** resistant granite → high ground; quartzite → the highest peak in Leong's case; shale and schist → low rounded hills; **permeable limestone → steep-sided hills**; ❗ the variable is **permeability**, not hardness. 🇮🇳 **Deccan Trap** basic extrusive fissure-fed; **Closepet granite** acid plutonic. ❌ Deccan as sedimentary — the steps are **successive flows**; *trap* from the Swedish for stair. **Owns 2022 GS-I Q4.** 🔁 Eruption style → vulcanism owner; black soil → soils owner.

## D. Secondary and Tertiary Rocks

**Three sedimentary origins:** **mechanical** (sandstone; **grit** coarser; **conglomerate** rounded; **breccia** angular; clay, shale, mudstone) · **organic** — **calcareous** (limestone, chalk, from corals and shellfish) and **carbonaceous** (peat, lignite, coal) · **chemical** (rock salt, gypsum, potash, nitrates). **By agent:** water → **marine, lacustrine, riverine**; wind → **loess**; ice → **moraines**. Desiccation cracks = sub-aerial riverine exposure. **Loess:** **coherent but not cemented**, **permeable**, **absence of stratification proves aeolian origin**, **absent in the equatorial tropics**; type case northern China. **Conglomerate vs breccia = distance of transport.** Metamorphic products: **phyllite, quartzite, schist, slate**; **gneiss** = quartz-feldspar banding; **slate** = slaty cleavage. Parent **recoverable** for marble and quartzite, **often not** for schist and gneiss. **Four chronology devices:** stratification and **superposition** · **fossils** (none in igneous; none in unfossiliferous Purana beds) · **unconformity** = deposition → deformation → uplift → **erosion** → renewed deposition, i.e. **time without rock** · **cross-cutting relations**, the only device that works in crystalline terrain. ⚠️ These give **relative** order; absolute ages are **quoted with their author**. 🇮🇳 **Vindhyan unconformity** (marine, calcareous, folded below; fluviatile, horizontal, undisturbed above); **Khondolite** (Eastern Ghats name, from the **Khond** tribes of Koraput and Bolangir; **Bengal Gneiss**, **Midnapur**) and **Charnockite** (**Job Charnock**'s Kolkata tombstone).

## E. Crustal Movement and Orogenic Chronology

**Compression → folding; tension → faulting.** Fold progression: simple (**anticline/syncline**) → **asymmetric** → **overfold** → **recumbent** → **overthrust**, the over-riding sheet a **nappe**; fold mountains = **mountains of elevation**. **Horst** = block left standing as ground on **either side subsides**; **steep faulted edges, scarp slopes, almost level summit** — **Hunsruck, Vosges, Black Forest**. ❗ **Block mountains and rift valleys are attributed to TENSION.** **Four classes:** fold · block · **volcanic, mountains of accumulation** (Fuji, Mayon, Merapi, Agung, Cotopaxi) · **residual, mountains of denudation** (Monadnock; Scottish Highlands, Scandinavia, **Deccan Plateau**). **Plateau types:** **tectonic** blocks (Deccan), *tilted* (**Meseta**), *faulted* (**Harz**) · **intermont** (**Tibetan**, **Bolivian**) · **volcanic** (**Antrim**, NW **Deccan**, **Columbia-Snake**). **Nine orogenies**; Pre-Cambrian **600-3,500 Ma**; **Caledonian ~320 Ma** (Scandinavia, Scotland, represented in North America); **Hercynian ~240 Ma** (Urals, Pennines, Welsh Highlands, Harz, **Appalachians**, Siberian and Chinese plateaux); **Alpine ~30 Ma** (Alps, **Himalayas**, **Andes**, Rockies). ❗ A second source dates the Alps ~65 Ma — irreconcilable; teach the **ordinal** claim, attach a date only with its source. **World map legend:** Alpine | Hercynian | Caledonian | **Pre-Cambrian shields** — class predicts height, sharpness, seismicity and denudation together. 🇮🇳 **Aravali** relict, folded at the **close of the Archaean**, **Delhi ridge**, buried toward Haridwar, **Chatterjee: Algonkian 600-700 Ma**; **Satpura = horst**; **Palghat Gap = rift between two parallel faults**. **Owns 2024 Prelims Q10.**

## F. Continental Drift

**Wegener** — meteorologist, polar explorer, astronomer, geologist — **first comprehensive case 1914**. **Pangaea** (*pan* + *gaea*) in **Panthalassa** (*pan* + *thalassa*); fragments into **Laurasia** and **Gondwana**. **Five evidence classes:** jig-saw fit (**better at the shelf edge** — coasts are erosional) · geological continuity on reassembly · identical terrestrial fossils on separated southern continents · palaeoclimatic anomaly (glacial deposits in present tropics; coal in present high latitudes) · **palaeomagnetism**, whose logic is **comparative** — divergent apparent polar wander paths with a fixed pole mean the **continents** moved. **Two proposed forces:** centrifugal **pole-fleeing**; **tidal force of Moon and Sun** — both **inadequate**. ❗ **Rejected for want of a mechanism, not because the evidence was false.** Secondary criticisms: the **'wakes'/'tracks'** objection (dissolved once the floor itself moves) and **selective use of evidence**. **Palaeomagnetic support 1950s-60s**; **absorbed into plate tectonics 1967** — fifty-three years. ❗ **Spreading (1960) ≠ plate tectonics (1967).** 🇮🇳 India in **Gondwana**; **Talchir series, Odisha**, glacial, **boulders matching Shimla, Hazara and the Salt Range** — tropical India carries a glacial record. **Owns 2025 Prelims Q25.**

## G. Palaeomagnetism and Sea-Floor Spreading

A cooling lava **locks in direction and polarity** → **apparent polar wander paths** and a **reversal sequence**. 🔁 The field, the geodynamo and the magnetosphere are the planetary topic's; this topic owns the **rock record**. **Ridge system first plotted 1928, *Meteor* oceanographers**; **Ring of Fire — Hugo Benioff, 1940**; ridges rise to ~**3,000 m**; **Mid-Atlantic Ridge above sea level in Iceland**; **shearing/neutral margins — San Andreas**. **Sea-floor spreading — H. Harry Hess, Princeton, 1960**, on **asthenospheric convection**. **Four observations:** **symmetrical** magnetic stripes · **age** increases from the axis · **sediment thickness** increases from the axis · **trenches with inclined earthquake zones** consume old floor; plus the floor is geologically **young** against continental shields. ❗ **Creation balances destruction — the Earth is not expanding**; this answers the 'tracks' objection. ⚠️ **Symmetry** is the decisive signature, not mere parallelism. What changed between 1914 and 1967 was the **model**, not the evidence. 🇮🇳 **Bay of Bengal and Arabian Sea came into being in the Cretaceous or early Tertiary after Gondwanaland disintegrated** — the sea is younger than the land. ❗ **No spreading rate from memory.** **Owns 2018 Prelims Q57**, limited to the recording mechanism.

## H. Plate Tectonics and the Ocean-Basin Cycle

**Seven major plates and several minor ones**; ❗ no precise total. **Shields oldest and most stable.** Oceanic crust ~**5 km**, continental **30-40 km** — thin, dense, ageing oceanic lithosphere subducts; thick buoyant continental lithosphere resists. **Divergent/constructive:** crust **created**; ridge (~**3,000 m**) or rift; **shallow-focus**; **fissure basaltic**. **Convergent ocean/continent:** trench + fold chain + **explosive andesitic** + **deep-focus** — **Andes**. **Convergent ocean/ocean:** the **older, denser** plate subducts → trench + **island arc** + back-arc sea. **Convergent continent/continent:** crust **thickened, not destroyed**; great fold mountains, high plateau; ❗ **little or no volcanism** — 🇮🇳 **Himalaya**. **Transform/conservative:** neither created nor destroyed; strike-slip; shallow but destructive; **no arc** — **San Andreas**. **Wilson rail:** **East African Rift → Red Sea and Gulf of Aden → Atlantic → Pacific → Mediterranean → Himalaya (closed Tethys)** — all six **simultaneously** today. **Passive (Atlantic-type)** margins inside a plate: wide shelves, thick wedges, aseismic → **offshore hydrocarbons**. **Active (Pacific-type)**: narrow shelves, trenches, arcs, seismic and volcanic. ⚠️ The cycle is an **ordering, not a timetable**; rifts fail, basins stall. 🇮🇳 **Tethys** in the Upper Carboniferous linked **east to the Pacific via China** and **west to the Atlantic via Afghanistan, Iran, Asia Minor and the present Mediterranean**; India closes one ocean in the north while two open to its south and west. **Owns 2025 GS-I Q16.**

## I. Isostasy and India's Three Structural Provinces

**Isostasy** = buoyant equilibrium of lighter crust on denser substratum. **Airy:** similar density, **different root depth** — higher mountain, **deeper root**. **Pratt:** **different density** above a **common depth of compensation**. ❗ The **Himalayan root argument is an Airy argument**; name the model. **Adjustment is observable:** post-glacial rebound; delta subsidence under sediment load (🔁 coastal owner). **Orogeny:** basin sedimentation → compression → folding and thrusting → thickening → **isostatic uplift** → **denudation**. **Three-fold hierarchy:** ancient shields · old block/relict mountains · young fold mountains — India holds all three. **Three geological regions:** Peninsular (**including Meghalaya and Kuchchh-Kathiawar**) · extra-Peninsular (Himalayas and eastern extensions, **including the Andaman and Nicobar Islands**) · the **Indo-Gangetic plain**. ⚠️ **Some scholars recognise only two divisions.** ✅ **"The Indian Peninsula has not undergone marine submergence since the Cambrian period"**; **one of the oldest land-masses**; **open senile topography**; **little affected by tectonic forces**. Extra-Peninsula: **originated in the Tethys Sea**; **most youthful relief on earth**; **prone to devastating earthquakes**; **"a weak and flexible portion of the earth's crust which has been folded, faulted and overthrust."** Plain: **flat, featureless, monotonous**. **One causal story:** collision → foredeep → infill. 🇮🇳 **Meghalaya is geologically Peninsular**, a **faulted/detached extension**. **Owns 2026 Prelims Q34.**

## J. India's Ladder I — Archaean and Dharwar

**Fourfold scheme:** **Archaean → Purana → Dravidian → Aryan**, after Holland and the GSI, as locked by the Advanced owner. ❗ **Divergence:** a second source places **Aravalli, Dharwar, Cuddapah, Vindhyan, Meghalaya Plateau and Mikir Hills inside an "Archaean System"** and uses "Purana" for the Pre-Cambrian **era**; name the divergence, do not harmonise. ❗ Dana applied "**Archaean**" to pre-Cambrian rock structure — **no date used**. **Archaean:** **cooling and solidification of the upper crust ~4,000 Ma**; **all azoic**; thoroughly crystalline, contorted, faulted, foliated, plutonic intrusions; **'Fundamental Complex' / 'Basement Complex'**; **gneiss over about two-thirds of the Peninsular surface**, granite to gabbro; schists of **mica, talc, hornblende, chlorite, epidote, sillimanite, graphite**; in the extra-Peninsula exposed along the Himalayas, **forming the backbone of the mountain system**. **Precambrian ≈ 86.7 per cent of Earth's history.** **Dharwar:** named from **Dharwar district, Karnataka**; **three cycles**, earliest **> 3,100 Ma**, latest **~2,300 Ma**, metamorphosed **~1,000 Ma**; ✅ **economically the most important rocks** — **iron ore, manganese, copper, lead, gold, quartzites, slates, mica**; **Kolar** gold. **Occurrence:** Dharwar-Bellary-Mysore · Ranchi-Hazaribagh · Balaghat-Rewa · Bastar, Dantewara, Kanker · Sundergarh-Keonjhar · Aravallis between Jaipur and Palanpur · **Rialo (Delhi) series → Makrana marble**. ❗ **Wadia:** the Dharwars are the most ancient metamorphosed **sedimentary** system, **as old as or older than the basement**. ❌ **Do not collapse Dharwar into "all Archaean rocks."** **Three gneisses:** **Bengal** (thinly foliated; Midnapur; **Khondolite**, **Khond** tribes) · **Bundelkhand** (coarse, granite-like, **quartz veins**) · **Nilgiri** (**Charnockite**, **Job Charnock**). Archaean = **"repositories of the mineral wealth of India"**; sixteen-mineral legend. 📰🔁 **National Critical Mineral Mission, 29 January 2025**, ₹**16,300 crore** over seven years, ~₹**18,000 crore** further, **1,200** GSI projects, **30** minerals — **routed, not taught**; the single Topic-02 sentence is that strategy is constrained by **where the Archaean and Dharwar outcrop**.

## K. India's Ladder II — Purana Basins and the Dravidian Gap

**"Purana" replaces "Proterozoic"**; divisions **Cuddapah** and **the Vindhyas**; Advanced owner dates the group **1400-600 Ma**. **Unfossiliferous** clays, slates, quartzites, sandstones, limestones in **great synclinal basins**; up to **6,100 m** in southern India; **5,200 m** along the Aravalli axis **Delhi to Idar (Gujarat)**; ❗ **separated from the Dharwars by a great unconformity**. **Cuddapah:** irregular **crescent, concave toward the coast, ~45,000 sq km** → **compression from the concave side, where high mountains once stood**. **Lower Vindhyan 1,300-1,100 Ma: marine, calcareous, tectonically folded.** **Upper Vindhyan 1,000-600 Ma: fluviatile, gently lying, undisturbed horizontal strata.** Break **prominent in the north, almost absent in southern Mewar, Chittaurgarh and the Son valley**. Upper encloses **two diamond-bearing horizons → Panna and Golconda**; otherwise **devoid of metalliferous minerals**, yielding **building stone, flagstones, ornamental stone, limestone, pure glass-making sand, some coal**; extent **over 1,00,000 sq km**. ✅ **Spate distinguished the Vindhyan rocks from the Vindhyan hills.** ✅ **Great Boundary Fault: Vindhyan System | Aravallis, about 800 km.** 🔁 Vindhyan red sandstone monuments — **Qutab Minar, Humayun's Tomb, Fatehpur Sikri, Agra Fort, Red Fort, Jama Masjid, Birla Mandir, Sanchi Stupa** — named once, routed. **Dravidian = Palaeozoic**, ✅ **"conspicuous by their absence in the Peninsular India except for one or two small patches of lower Permian age near Umaria"**; **Cambrian and Devonian fossil-bearing rocks unknown in the Peninsula**. ⚠️ **The absence is the evidence** for the Cambrian claim. ❌ **Gondwana is not Dravidian.** **Periods:** **Cambrian 600** (*Cambria* = Wales) — **Haimanta** of Spiti, **1,600 m**; **saline series** of the Salt Range, **900 m** over salt marl · **Ordovician 500** (**Ordovices**) · **Silurian 440** (**Silures**) — **Lidar anticline** · **Devonian 400** — **Muth quartzite**, **900 m**, **devoid of fossils** · **Carboniferous 350** — "coal bearing"; ✅ **Everest is Upper Carboniferous limestone**. ❗ Not quoted: the Palaeozoic "to 24.5 Ma" terminal date; Pangaea's break-up placed in the Palaeozoic.

## L. The Aryan System I — Gondwana, Deccan Trap, Coal

**Nine-step Aryan spine:** Tethys geosyncline in the **Upper Carboniferous**, linked **east to the Pacific via China**, **west to the Atlantic via Afghanistan, Iran, Asia Minor, the present Mediterranean** → volcanic activity in the Kashmir Himalayas → **fissuring of Gondwanaland and India's northward drift** → **Deccan Trap** → **Arabian Sea and Bay of Bengal** → **Tertiary mountain-building → Himalayas** → India's present shape → **Pleistocene Ice Age** → evolution and spread of man. ❗ **Pangaea assembled in the late Palaeozoic, broke up in the Mesozoic.** **Gondwana:** **continental, fluviatile, lacustrine** deposits in **geosynclinal troughs on an ancient plateau surface**; **loaded troughs subsided** → ~**6,000 m** of flat strata from the start of the **Permian, ~250 Ma**. ⚠️ A **shallow basin that kept sinking**, not a deep basin slowly filled. Name from the **Kingdom of the Gonds**. **Four belts:** **Damodar** (Jharkhand) · **Mahanadi** (Chhattisgarh and Odisha) · **southern Madhya Pradesh** · **Godavari troughs, Nagpur to the delta**; extra-Peninsular **Kashmir, Darjeeling, Sikkim**. **Lower Gondwana = Talcher + Panchet + Damuda**; **Talcher** from Dhenkanal district, Odisha; **Damuda** seams **Raniganj, Jharia, Karanpura, Bokaro, Singrauli, Korba, Pench, Talcher, Singareni**; **Jhingurda ~131 m = thickest seam in India**. ❗ **Singareni is in the Godavari valley.** ✅ **~98 per cent of India's coal reserves** — a **structural proportion**, attributed, ❗ not a current reserve figure. ❌ Coal in the Deccan; **Tertiary coal is important in the north-east**. **Mesozoic:** **Triassic 280-225** (Hazara to Nepal; ❗ no thickness) · **Jurassic 180** (**Kachchh 190 × 64 km**, **Jaisalmer**) · **Cretaceous 135-70** (*creta* = chalk; ✅ **"no other system is so widely distributed in India"**; **Bagh Beds**, **Lametas**). **Deccan Trap:** end-**Cretaceous** to early **Eocene**; **~10 lakh sq km** of **extremely mobile fissure-fed basalt**, **covering fully the pre-existing topography**; ❗ **denudation has halved it to ~5 lakh sq km**; flows **0.5-36 m**; **Mumbai ~3,000 m · Kachchh 800 m · southern limit 600-800 m · Amarkantak 150 m** — a **west-thickening wedge**; **Upper ~450 m** (many inter-trappeans and ash) · **Middle ~1,200 m** (ash above, practically devoid of inter-trappeans) · **Lower ~150 m** (inter-trappeans present, ash rare); **inter-trappean beds prove episodic eruption**; *trap* from the **Swedish for stair**; ✅ **Spate prefers "Deccan Lava"**. 📰 **GSI Field Season 2025-26 concluded** — PIB / Ministry of Mines, **2 April 2026**, GSI's **175th year**: **458** projects, **230** critical-mineral, **92** rare-earth, **80** reports for auction, **~22,000 sq km** geological and **28,000 sq km geochemical mapping in the Deccan Traps**, **naturally occurring hydrogen in the Andaman and Nicobar Islands**, drone magnetic surveys, AI-driven targeting. 🔁 Counts and auctions **routed**.

## M. The Aryan System II — Tertiary, Shiwaliks, Quaternary

✅ **"The Tertiary is the most significant period in India's geological history because the Himalayas were born and India's present form came into being in this period"**; the **Age of the Mammals**. **Eocene ~60 Ma:** Jammu to **Shimla and Mussoorie**; **Rajasthan lignite**; **Jaintia series** of Meghalaya; **Barail series**, Surma valley and Naga Hills. **Oligocene-Lower Miocene 40-25 Ma: very poorly developed because Tertiary outcrops suffered heavy denudation**; **Murree series**, a **25-40 km** belt, merging east into **Dagshai-Kasauli (Sirmur)**, ~**2,600 m**. **Shiwalik System, Middle Miocene to Lower Pleistocene, 14-0.2 Ma**, up to ~**5,000 m**; **mammalian fossils first discovered near Haridwar in 1839**; **Dupi Tila → Lakhimpur and Digboi** oil fields; **Upper 1,800-2,750 m · Middle 1,800-2,500 m · Lower 1,200-1,500 m**. ⚠️ The Shiwaliks are the **range's own debris**, folded by a southward-advancing front. **Three upheaval phases (source-attached):** **Eocene ~65 Ma** → **Greater Himalayas**; **mid-Miocene ~45 Ma** → **Lesser**; **post-Pliocene ~1.4 Ma** → **Shiwaliks**; ✅ **still rising**. ❌ Himalaya volcanic; ❌ Himalaya as only young Tertiary sedimentary rock. ✅ **The Great Indo-Gangetic-Brahmaputra Plain had not figured at all until the Quaternary**; its floor is a **synclinal basin — the foredeep, a downwarp of the Himalayan foreland of variable depth, formed concomitantly with the rise of the Himalayas**. **Recent began ~12,000 years ago**; **de Terra and Paterson — four glacial, three inter-glacial**; sea level ~**100 m lower** in glacials. **Karewas** — flat-topped terraces of clays, sands and silts with lenticles of old deltaic-fan conglomerate on the **Pir Panjal** flanks, **second inter-glacial**. ❗ ***Bhangar*** **= older alluvium, Middle-to-Upper Pleistocene**; ***khadar*** **= newer alluvium, Upper Pleistocene**, flood-prone lower valleys — **stratigraphic units, not soils**. **Three ice ages:** **Dharwar** (conglomerates near **Kaldurg**) · **Gondwana** (**Talchir**, boulders matching **Shimla, Hazara, the Salt Range**) · **Pleistocene** (Himalayan glaciation to **1,800 m**, drift and terminal moraines to **1,400 m** in Kashmir, ancient moraines ~**1,650 m**, lakes **Kailash Kund, Gulmarg, Sheshnag, Kaunsarnag**); ✅ **the Peninsula shows no Pleistocene glaciation**. **Coast:** ~**6,100 km**, uniform and regular; **Suess** attributes straightness to the **disruption and faulting of Gondwanaland during the Cretaceous**; **few sites for good natural harbours**; 🔁 coastline **measurement and convention** → planetary topic. **Emergence/submergence:** submerged forest **6-12 m** below sea level east of **Mumbai island** · lignite bed **73 m** below ground at **Puducherry** · submerged **Dwarka** · the **1819 Rann of Kachchh earthquake**, submerging **5,180 sq km** with **Sindree fort** and elevating ~**1,550 sq km** · raised beaches **30-45 m** on the **Makran** coast · the steep **Sahyadri** face.

## N. Structure, Relief and Resource

✅ **Why structure matters:** land-use planning · irrigation potential · groundwater quality and quantity · transport and communication alignment · understanding of earthquakes, volcanoes, landslides and floods. ✅ **The controlled contrast:** the alluvial Indo-Gangetic plain is **extremely useful for agriculture but almost completely devoid of mineral deposits**; the Peninsular plateau, **especially Chotanagpur**, is **very rich**. **Six divisions** with structural character, relief/drainage consequence and land-use consequence: Himalaya · Northern Plain · Peninsular Plateau · the two Ghats · Coastal Plains · Islands. ❌ **The Ghats are not a matched pair:** Western = continuous **faulted escarpment** and the Peninsula's **water divide**; Eastern = **discontinuous, lower, repeatedly breached**. 🇮🇳 **Aravali:** relict of the **world's oldest mountain**, folded at the **close of the Archaean**, **800 km Delhi to Palanpur**, **Guru Sikhar 1,722 m**, **Mt Abu 1,158 m**, **Delhi ridge**, buried toward Haridwar, **Chatterjee: Algonkian 600-700 Ma**. 🇮🇳 **Satpura = horst** (*sat* + *pura*); **Dhupgarh 1,350 m** on the **Mahadev Hills**; **Astamba Dongar 1,325 m**; **Amarkantak 1,127 m**. 🇮🇳 **Western Ghats / Sahyadri: 1,600 km**, Tapi valley to Kanniyakumari; **Anai Mudi 2,695 m**, **Doda Betta 2,637 m**, **Makurti 2,554 m**; **Palghat Gap 24-30 km wide at 75-300 m**, a probable **rift valley between two parallel faults**. 🇮🇳 **Eastern Ghats:** **"a chain of highly broken and detached hills"**, Mahanadi to the Vaigai, **neither structural unity nor physiographic continuity**, ✅ **the name is a misnomer**; **Spate: "Eastern Hills"**; **Eastern Ghats / Cuddapah Ranges / Tamilnad Hills**; **Mahendra Giri 1,501 m**. **Plateau sub-divisions:** **Malwa** (two drainage systems; ***Pat*** laterite) · **Baghelkhand** · **Chotanagpur** (**over 87,000 sq km**; **Hazaribagh** *Gorus* **1,119 m / 1,142 m**; **Ranchi 600 m**; **Parasnath 1,366 m**) · **Meghalaya** (**Garo 900 m, Khasi-Jaintia 1,500 m, Mikir 700 m**; **Shillong 1,961 m**) · **Deccan** (~**five lakh sq km**: Maharashtra, Karnataka with ***Malnad*** and ***Maidan***, Telangana) · **Chhattisgarh Plain**, a **saucer-shaped depression on nearly horizontal Cuddapah limestone and shale**, ✅ **"the only plain worth the name"** in the Peninsula. ❗ **Three limits:** association not determination · multiple causation at every node · **no peak heights, plain widths, plateau areas, coastline lengths or sub-division counts from memory**. **Owns 2023 Prelims Q9.** 🔁 Deccan resource potential, Gondwanaland mining economics and basalt carbonation are **routed**.

## O. Mapwork and Answer Architecture

**Build order:** outline → **shield, granites, charnockites** → **Dharwar belts** → **Purana (Cuddapah crescent, Vindhyan spread)** → **Gondwana four belts** → **Deccan and Rajmahal flows** → **Tertiary with Shiwaliks and Tipam** → **Quaternary** → **lineaments last**. ❗ **The order is the content.** **Composite legend (sixteen classes):** Quaternary · Shiwaliks and Tipam · Lower and Middle Miocene · Palaeocene · Coastal Tertiary · Rajmahal and Deccan Flows · Mesozoic · Mesozoic and Palaeozoic windows · Gondwana and Upper Palaeozoic · Vindhyan and Cuddapah · Purana and Lower Palaeozoic · **Malani Volcanics** · Dharwar · Granites of all ages · Unclassified crystallines · **Charnockites**. **Nine assets:** M1 world structural divisions · M2 plates · M3 India composite · M4 eight single-system overlays · M5 four-panel palaeogeographic sequence · M6 Archaean with mineral legend · M7 Deccan thickness profile · M8 hill-range inventory · M9 lineaments (**Great Boundary Fault**, **Main Central Thrust**). ❗ Individual series names from the series-and-faults list are **not quoted as decisive**. **Directive decoding:** *characteristics and types* → six criteria + **two axes** + **forms** · *changes in continents and ocean basins* → mechanism **plus** the life cycle with living examples · *evidences of drift* → five classes with their logic · *formation of fold mountains* → convergence → shortening → thickening → isostatic uplift · *rock type and human activity* → petrology → weathering → soil/aquifer/mineral → land use · *isostasy* → **Airy against Pratt** plus observable adjustment. **Tectonic ≠ geomorphic:** tectonic = age, stability, deformation, marine history; geomorphic = relief, surface form, structural style. **Evidence ladder: 10** = thesis + 3-4 steps + Indian example + qualification · **15** = + a second **mode** of evidence and an evaluation paragraph · **20** = + a second **axis** and a **graded verdict**. ❗ Extra marks buy a **new kind** of content. **Four evidence units:** ocean-basin life cycle · tectonic setting fixes hydrocarbons and earthquakes · rock genesis governs surface geography · India's mineral and agricultural geographies do not coincide, and the reason is lithological. **Number protocol: A** freely · **B** with source and date · ❗ **C never from memory** — spreading rates, plate counts beyond seven major, unattributed collision dates, current production or reserves, Indian crustal thicknesses, sub-division counts, Triassic thicknesses, the current coastline measurement. **Four pre-writing checks: directive · budget · number class · boundary.**

## Part 4 — Declared exclusions, with the reason for each

| Excluded | Reason |
|---|---|
| Era-period-epoch geological time-scale tables | Low-yield memorisation. The single proportion — **Precambrian ≈ 86.7 per cent of Earth's history** — does the work a table would do, at a fraction of the length |
| Exhaustive mineral-occurrence district inventories | Retained in compressed form at L10 and L14; the full district lists are transcription without analytical return, and several administrative names in them are superseded |
| Book-era production and reserve tables | Derived from mid-2000s compilations and stale. The **structural proportion** (Gondwana ≈ 98 per cent of coal reserves) is retained with its attribution; no production or reserve table appears |
| Volcano and earthquake case files; eruption styles; seismic zonation; tsunami | 🔁 Owned by the vulcanism-and-earthquakes topic. This file uses seismic waves **only as evidence for internal structure** and the fissure flow **only as an emplacement mechanism** |
| Himalayan physiographic sub-division detail | 🔁 Owned by the physiography topic. Only the three dated upheaval phases, the Shiwalik stratigraphy and the foredeep are taken |
| Mineral chemistry and crystallography beyond the named rock-forming minerals | Out of scope for a General Studies answer |
| Compiled four-option objective corpora | Prohibited in concept-check mode; hard objective and option-elimination practice belongs to the separate workbook workflow |
| Solved previous-year model answers | Prohibited in a live-session file. Year, paper, number, conceptual demand and answer **approach** are supplied instead |
| Any answer letter, truth marking or elimination cue for P3-P7 | Answer-neutrality. Two Set-A keys are held locally and the 2026 key is provisional; none is recorded, inferred or hinted at |
| An Indian crustal-thickness figure for the Peninsula or the Himalaya | Not carried by any source available to this edition; the structural statement is given instead |
| A Triassic thickness for Kashmir | The source passage is internally inconsistent on this value |
| A date for the introduction of the term "Archaean" | The locally printed date is impossible; the undated attribution to Dana is used |
| The Palaeozoic terminal date as printed in one local source | Almost certainly a typesetting slip; the internally consistent period-by-period dates are used instead |
| A single asserted date for the Alpine orogeny | Two sources disagree irreconcilably at one-line granularity; the ordinal claim is taught and a figure is given only with its source |
| Individual series names from the series-and-faults list | Recovered from a page whose two-column layout renders unreliably; only the **Main Central Thrust**, independently corroborated, is carried forward |
| India's coastline measurement and the convention behind it | 🔁 Owned by the planetary and location topic. The ~6,100 km figure appears here **only** as a book-era structural statement supporting the straightness argument |
| Resource potential of the Deccan Trap; Gondwanaland mining economics; critical-mineral policy architecture; basalt carbon sequestration; geoheritage; mining's ecological cost; MMDR amendments and block auctions | 🔁 Owned respectively by the mineral-and-energy-resources topic, the science-and-technology folder, the environment folder and the economy folder. Each is named once at the point of contact and routed |
| The permanently excluded learner-package tree | Not read, not searched, not cited, under the governing source-exclusion rule |

## Scope, boundary and typing

- A **Mains-focused** edition of Geography Topic 02, addressing **GS-I directly** because Geography is a named GS-I owner. It preserves the integrated topic — the world half and the India half in **one file** — across fifteen lessons, with Lessons 1-8 each closing on an assigned India hook, Lesson 9 as the bridge and Lessons 10-14 carrying India.
- **Ownership stated precisely.** Within "Important Geophysical phenomena", this topic owns **seismic waves solely as evidence for internal structure** and the **fissure eruption solely as the Deccan's emplacement mechanism**; earthquakes, eruption styles, zonation and tsunami belong to the vulcanism-and-earthquakes topic. Within the natural-resources clause it owns the **lithological substrate only**; distribution, reserves and mining economics belong to the mineral-and-energy-resources topic.
- **Typing.** ✅ marks a statement carried in a named source — a canonical owner, a book page listed below, or an official instrument. ⚠️ marks analytical inference, evaluation, standard synthesis or a flagged limitation. 📰 marks a dated current item. 🔁 marks an ownership routing. ❗ marks a hard prohibition or a discipline an examiner can check. 🔑 marks a trap. 🇮🇳 marks an India-specific consequence.
- **Page conventions, recorded so that no citation here is mistaken for another edition's pagination.** Majid Husain, *Indian & World Geography*, uses **chapter-relative folios** of the form `<chapter>.<page>` with no continuous book-wide numbering; this edition cites them as printed folios with the local-scan page beside them where needed. D.R. Khullar's local scan carries **two printed pages per scan page**, stacked and rotated; printed page numbers are cited. Majid Husain, *Geography of India*, is a **partial 160-page scan** with chapter-relative folios and one printed page per scan page; only printed 1.1-1.15 and the Chapter 2 contents list are cited. ❗ **G.C. Leong's local scan has missing pages and a non-uniform offset**, so Leong is cited **by local-scan page only**, and a printed folio is given only where directly visible on the page.
- **Pages actually rendered and read, which bound quotation-grade fidelity.** Majid *Geography of India*: scan pp.3-11, 13, 14, 16, 17, 19-23. D.R. Khullar: scan pp.3, 4, 5, 37, 38, 39, 40, 41, 42, 58, 59 (with 43, 45, 57, 61 rendered). G.C. Leong: scan pp.16-22 and 30. Majid *Indian & World Geography*: pp.23-35, 345 and 348-349 via normalised text extraction rather than rendering.
- **Answer-neutrality.** No answer letter, truth marking, elimination cue or corrective mapping appears for any objective demand, including the two whose official Set-A keys are held locally and the 2026 demand whose key is provisional. No solved previous-year model answer appears, and no replacement solved-practice workbook was created. All **six** final-arc Mains practice questions and all **fifteen** lesson-local practice questions are **original** and are marked as such.
- **Current-affairs discipline.** Exactly **one file-level current linkage** is carried: the conclusion of the Geological Survey of India's Field Season 2025-26, at Lesson 12, with one permitted reference in Lesson 14's closing paragraph. The National Critical Mineral Mission docket at Lesson 10 is labelled **verification of an existing static anchor**, not a second linkage, and is confined to one routed sentence. No current item is attached to Lessons 1-9, 11, 13 or 15, and their pre-teach records say so truthfully. ❗ For a topic whose subject matter is four thousand million years old, an honest nil return is the correct result and a stretched linkage would be a worse failure than an absent one.
- **Vector store.** The local vector database was not reachable during this pass. It is optional and non-blocking under the governing source rule, it is not a manifest category, and this edition does not depend on it.

## Corrections and normalisations applied to source material, declared

| # | Source wording or content | Correction applied |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | **Scheme conflict.** Majid *Geography of India* places the **Aravalli, Dharwar, Cuddapah, Vindhyan, the Meghalaya Plateau and the Mikir Hills inside an "Archaean System"**, calls the Pre-Cambrian era "the Purana" and attributes the Indian time scale to T.S. Holland; Khullar, followed by the Advanced owner, makes **Purana = Cuddapah + Vindhyan, separate from the Archaean** | **Khullar's fourfold scheme is taught**, as locked by the Advanced owner. Majid *Geography of India* is cited for **occurrence, etymology and economic detail only**. ❗ The divergence is **stated explicitly at Lesson 10** and is not silently harmonised |
| 2 | "The term 'Archaean' was introduced by **J.D. Dana in 1782**" | ❗ **The date is not used.** Dana is credited, undated, with first applying "Archaean" to pre-Cambrian rock structure |
| 3 | "The Palaeozoic Era extends from 570 million years ago to **24.5 million years ago**" | ❗ **The terminal date is not quoted.** Khullar's internally consistent period-by-period dates are used instead |
| 4 | "During this period **Pangaea broke and the Tethys Sea came into existence**", placed in the Palaeozoic | **Assembly and break-up are taught as separate events on opposite sides of the Permo-Triassic boundary**, following the nine-step Aryan chronology, which orders them correctly |
| 5 | **Singareni** placed in the "Satpura basin, Madhya Pradesh" | Placed in the **Godavari valley belt**, which is also where Khullar's own four-belt list puts it |
| 6 | Book-era statistics throughout both Indian volumes, drawn from mid-2000s compilations, with pre-reorganisation state and district names | ❗ **All production and reserve tables excluded.** Current administrative names are used — Telangana, Chhattisgarh, Odisha, Uttarakhand, Puducherry — and the substitution is declared here |
| 7 | Majid *Indian & World Geography* printed 10.5 geographic-extremes block, including an incorrect elevation and a sovereignty-sensitive territorial description | ❗ **Out of this topic's span and not imported.** Already normalised in the adjacent Topic-01 lane |
| 8 | "Diamond from carbon" offered as a metamorphic example | The **coal → graphite** pair is taught, with the Core owner's own softening that it is not a universal one-step rule; ❗ the pair is **not extended to diamond** |
| 9 | The Alps dated to "about 65 million years ago" in one source against Leong's Alpine orogeny at "about 30 Ma" | ❗ **Not reconcilable at one-line granularity.** The **ordinal** claim is taught — the Alpine is the most recent of the three named orogenies — and a date is given **only with its source attached**. The conflict is disclosed at Lesson 5 |
| 10 | Khullar's Triassic paragraph gives the Middle Trias of Kashmir as both "275 m thick" and "only 100 m thick" in a single sentence | ❗ **No Triassic thickness is quoted.** Only the Triassic's distribution claim is retained |
| 11 | Leong's measurements are **imperial and mid-century** — miles, feet, degrees Fahrenheit | Quoted **as Leong's**, in his units, explicitly flagged as a mid-century textbook statement; ❗ not silently converted and not presented as current geophysics |
| 12 | A page-offset convention carried forward from the adjacent lane, recording a uniform `printed = scan − 6` for G.C. Leong | ❗ **Corrected.** Direct rendering shows Chapter 2 heading the scan at p.17, with visible folios implying a **non-uniform offset and missing pages**. Leong is therefore cited **by local-scan page only** throughout this file |
| 13 | The series-and-faults list on a two-column page whose extraction is garbled, with an adjacent page carrying an empty text layer | ❗ **No individual series name from that list is quoted as decisive.** Only the **Main Central Thrust** is carried forward, and the **Great Boundary Fault** is taken from an independently read page |
| 14 | Khullar's "Ice Ages in India" passage contains one basin name that renders ambiguously at the resolution available | The three ice ages and the glacial-lake **category** are taught; only the four lake names that render cleanly — **Kailash Kund, Gulmarg, Sheshnag, Kaunsarnag** — are quoted |
| 15 | The enumerated characteristic lists for the three rock families (ten igneous, nine sedimentary, seven metamorphic) | ⚠️ Recovered at **summary rather than quotation grade**. The numbered lists are **not reproduced as such**; the characteristics independently supported by the canonical owners and the readable source text are given instead, and the limitation is stated in the lesson itself |

## Open uncertainties carried forward

1. **Three of the four named books carry no usable text layer, and no OCR engine is installed in this environment.** Only Majid Husain's *Indian & World Geography* is text-searchable. Evidence from G.C. Leong, D.R. Khullar and Majid Husain's *Geography of India* was recovered by rendering pages to image and reading them visually, so **quotation-grade fidelity exists only for the pages enumerated above**.
2. **Pages not rendered were not read.** Specifically: Leong beyond scan p.22; figure captions on unrendered Khullar pages; and several *Geography of India* pages that were rendered but only partially read. ❗ This file therefore never asserts that a book is **silent** on a point.
3. **Majid Husain's *Geography of India* is a partial 160-page scan** of a larger work. Chapter and section numbering beyond Chapter 2 could not be verified, and citation is confined to printed 1.1-1.15 and the Chapter 2 contents list.
4. **Answer keys.** The 2018 and 2023 Prelims keys are **unavailable locally**; the 2024 and 2025 Set-A keys are **physically present but deliberately not recorded or inferred**; the 2026 Set-A key is **provisional** and was not consulted. Answer-neutrality makes this moot for the edition, but it is recorded here.
5. **Official-page access.** The issuing body's press page for the Field Season 2025-26 item resists direct extraction in this environment, and the departmental mirror returns raw document bytes rather than parsable text. The item was therefore verified through **independent citing reports carrying the same figures**, and the citation here gives the **issuing body and the release date only**; ❗ no press-release identification number is quoted, because none was directly read.
6. **Precambrian and Proterozoic dating in the Indian literature is soft**, particularly for unfossiliferous sequences. Every such figure in this file is the **cited author's published value**, and the brackets should be treated as wide.
7. **The vector store was unreachable.** Optional and non-blocking; recorded once here as not used.
8. **Lane state.** This edition generates its own complete final arc and source manifest. It makes no edit to the index, to the Geography Mains generation rules, to the canonical owners or to the adjacent Topic-01 lane, and it asserts nothing about them beyond their existence as conventions it follows.
9. **Comparative magnitudes outside India.** Where a world-half comparison would require extents, thicknesses or ages for a non-Indian province — the Columbia-Snake, for instance — this edition holds no such data and **declines to assert a comparison of magnitude**, confining itself to process and form.
